Children of the Mental-Motive Type 

Children of this type have a wonderful aptitude for development. They require no urge to participate in sports and exercise; yet they will be content to study or perform the tasks required of them. They are easily trained, but care must be taken to keep them well balanced. Indulgence in certain indoor hobbies may lead them to neglect their physical welfare-not because they do not enjoy outdoor activity, but because they may prefer the other interest. Similarly, time must be allowed for their mental work. 

They should not be discouraged from the matters that interest them if such matters do not interfere too grealy with other affairs of importance.

Faults of the Mental-Motive Type 

Divided interest is a fault of the Mental-Motive Type. The fact that so many persons of this sort gain their success late in life is proof that their development was delayed; and for those who do succeed there are those who do not.

They are theorists, with practical desire; hence they often put into action plans which are not usable, and suffer as a consequence. 

They must learn to balance their work rather than to divide it; to make their Mental and Motive attributes harmonize rather than work in opposition.

Occupations for the Mental-Motive Type 

Virile writers-historical romancers-sports writers-these are typical of the Mental-Motive. Persons of this type are good journalists, and make capable advertising men.

They can succeed as professional men, particularly as specialists in new fields; they become architects, mechanical engineers, superintendents, and sales managers. 45

CHARACTER READING 

They are found as manufacturers, especially of new commodities. They become technical writers, and physical instructors. 

The present period of inventive progress offers many opportunities for those of the Mental-Motive Type. 

Triangular-Circular Form: Mental-Vital Type 

The Triangular-Circular Form shows a head and face with a definite inclination toward roundness, yet with the noticeable tapering at the bottom that indicates the Triangle. 

The body is round, rather pudgy, yet does not have the great fullness so evident in the Circular Form. At the same time, a person of the Mental-Vital Type may become quite corpulent in later age.

The Mental-Vital is the thinker who desires. He has excellent power of judgment, for he can plan and direct; he is capable of settling the affairs of others; and as a consultant he has no equal. 

People of this type use their brains to get results; they are practical-minded, analyzing why certain enterprises are successful, and they devote their thought to increased production. 

They are wise, intelligent money-makers; not mentally passive as are so many of the purely Vital Type; but alert, and constantly directing.

They are great advocates of efficient methods that reduce labor or manual operation. They like to see brain-work and ingenuity accomplish things. They are idealistic, and appreciate beauty and harmony. 

They have the Vital Type ability to pass fair-judgment; but they also have the quality of considering a matter carefully; hence their decisions are based upon knowledge and perception. 

They are interested in all sorts of subjects, and do not confine themselves to intellectual pursuits, but give the same consideration to everything that they regard of consequence. At the same time they are capable of deciding just how important or unimportant anything should be.

They are interested in all types of humanity, and seem to understand the other person's viewpoint. While they enjoy ease and comfort, they are moderate in every way, and can restrain themselves from any desire that they know may prove disastrous. 

Men of the Mental-Vital Type 

Men of the Mental-Vital Type are highly esteemed, and reach great heights, purely because other people rely on them. They 46

have an easy way of solving problems, and they possess a contagious optimism that is reassuring.

They establish themselves, and are careful before they make an important decision. In positions of trust, they are extremely careful, and their fundamental principle is to practice what they preach. They are tolerant, and understand the weaknesses of others, but they are firm in their decisions. 

Women of the Mental-Vital Type 

Women of the Mental-Vital Type are cheery, home-loving individuals who provide the best things for the household in a careful, sensible way. They are helpful to their husbands and their children, solving many problems for them. 

They are neighborly, but not gossipy; friendly, but not too intimate; they are the sort of people one will count on in an emergency. They have their own ideas and uphold them; but they are broad-minded and fair to all. 

Children of the Mental-Vital Type 

Children of this type are usually happy, and easy to handle. 

They will listen to reason, and can be taught to discriminate between those things that are right and wrong. 

They have a tendency to avoid physical exertion, but will understand the importance of exercise, and will not avoid outdoor recreation. They are popular among their playfellows, and are satisfied with a reasonable amount of pleasures. 

Faults of the Mental-Vital Type 

The chief fault of the Mental-Vital Type is inactivity of self. 

These people are directing; while they will argue reasonably that they should do certain things, and will do them, they have a tendency to do as little as possible-in fact just enough to satisfy their own minds.

They will be fond of sports and active pursuits, but will argue that there are others better fitted to participate in such matters, and will therefore neglect their own development. 

The mental in them will often predominate to an excessive extent; they will put off doing things until a more suitable time. 

There is nothing spectacular in their make-up; they do their work quietly and methodically, and if they are satisfied that they have done properly, they are oblivious to public opinion. 47

CHARACTER READING Occupations for the Mental-Vital Type 

In work, people of the Mental-Vital Type succeed in the higher branches for which the Vital Type is suited. 

They are capable judges, politicians, lawyers, and statesmen. 

They become leaders of industry-bankers, and financiers. They are students of production and management; they make fine theatrical producers, motion picture directors, and heads of large corporations.

Square-Circular Form: Motive-Vital Type 

This formation inclines toward the Circular, but with obvious traces of the squareness, particularly in the cheek-bones and the jaw. The body shows the physical solidity of the Square 

Form, but is inclined to stoutness, especially in persons of middle age.

The Motive-Vital Type persons want action, and it makes up their principal desire. Yet they also enjoy comfort in connection with it. As they gain the better things of life, this latter tendency reaches its ascendant. 

They are found in railroading, engineering, and other pursuits that attract the Motive Type; yet they are content to pass the reins over to someone else and retire. 

They are independent, and will not yield to argument; but being of friendly disposition they do not show animosity, and can be made to change their viewpoints if they are tactfully handled.

They take great pride in their accomplishments, and measure them in terms of commercial success. They undertake matters with determination, and feel that they are due for their share of any returns that they may bring.

They are drivers of men, but are quite willing that those whom they drive should be rewarded. They are exacting in their methods yet lenient and willing to grant reasonable requests. 

Men of the Motive-Vital Type 

Men of this type are athletic in their youth, and retain such interest as they grow older. They are seen as football and baseball coaches and managers.

They have the commercial instinct, and build up constantly, persevering in a single occupation and gaining considerable reputation; and they will overcome obstacles by their active 48

power. When they establish rules they will expect them to be obeyed; but they will generally make few rules. 

Women of the Motive-Vital Type 

Women of the Motive-Vital Type are happy and friendly toward others, but they are insistent that certain principles be enforced. Some things, to them, are entirely wrong. Their scruples are few but positive.

They are generally tolerant and easy to get along with, but when they champion a cause, they will stand for no interference. 

Children of the Motive-Vital Type 

In handling children of this type, it should be remembered that desire is inherent in them. Being physically active and happy in disposition, they enjoy play and will not want to study if they can help it.

But they are susceptible to reward, and cannot resist the lure of desire. They are the type who will listen to a cash offer if they do not smoke until they reach a certain age. They want their own way; but they may want something else more; and tactful handling makes them responsive.

Faults of the Motive-Vital Type 

Decadence is present in the Motive-Vital Type. Many men who were once quite athletic become corpulent and sluggish. 

Women who were active as girls become fond of comfort and amusement.

There is always this struggle in the Motive-Vital. They are not great thinkers; they do not create new ideas; but they have the habit of appropriating the methods of others, and putting them forth as their own.

They become egotistical, and fond of unrestrained pleasure. 

They boast of the things they have done—particularly in active lines and overlook the fact that they can do them no longer. 

They will yield to prejudice and narrow-mindedness; for they are quick to gain the failings of their type. Yet they are highly esteemed by those who understand them, and they are goodhearted and friendly when occasion arises. 

They are lured away from active pursuits by love of leisure, and are susceptible to temptation. They often work against their own interests, and make great mistakes of judgment. 49

CHARACTER READING Occupations for the Motive-Vital Type 

Being fond of outdoor life, persons of the Motive-Vital Type are excellent contractors, builders, railroad men, traction officials, and military officers. 

They are good superintendents, manufacturing managers, and they have sales ability. 

They are good managers of sports; trainers and coaches.

They are excellent referees and umpires of sporting events, having firmness with impartiality.

The Balanced Form: Mental-Motive-Vital 

Having discussed the three combined types, we must consider the triple type in which the qualities of Triangular, Square and 

Circular Formation are present.

Theoretically this is the Ideal Type: one in which Reason, Will and Desire are harmoniously blended. But persons who fit this exactly are very, very few.

The Ideal Type is a thinker who is active; and who has a fine perception of humanity, enjoying the fruits of brain-work and honest toil to a moderate degree.

He can succeed in almost any occupation; he will be esteemed by all whom he meets, and his good qualities will outnumber his faults. The woman of the Ideal Type will have grace, pleasantness of disposition, and intelligence. The child of the Ideal Type will be versatile and responsive; studious and fond of physical activity.

In reading Character one frequently sees persons who have head and face formation which indicate the Balanced Form. But close study will usually show that one of the three formations is subordinate to the other two. Hence Mental, Motive, or Vital traits are secondary. 

Vital Secondary is indicated by comparatively little fullness of the features-an oval face with the wide head and the square jaw more in evidence than the circle. The failure of this tpye to reach the balance is found in poor or under-developed commercial instinct and knowledge of values; and occasional lack of human understanding, displayed chiefly by seriousness of disposition, without joviality.

Motive Secondary is seen in the face which is full with the chin slightly pointed, and just the trace of straightness from the temples to the cheeks. 50

Here is a capacity for physical effort which is never given its full development. Athletes who fail to live up to expectations; women who find other matters more important than the outdoor occupations that they admit are valuable; children who are strong and capable yet who allow others to bully them are all lacking in their Motive strength. 

Mental Secondary has the circular head formation; the straight sides, and the slightly tapering chin; but the width of the head is no greater than that of the face. 

Here we have the sluggish thinker-one who has ideas and originality, but seldom displays these qualities. These people show occasional flashes of genius, and then lapse into action and pleasure. They provide expectations, but do not realize them. 

The Ideal Balanced Type is not mythical; there are some persons who display it, and they are easily recognized. Others come close to it, and are lacking only in minor details. The way to judge this type is by a negative process. Recognize the Ideal 

Balance; then try to see what is subordinate-Mental, Motive, or Vital. Whenever one of these factors seems to be secondary, make allowances accordingly.

Do not expect the Mental Secondary person to display concentrated continued thinking with ideas that are constantly sound. 

Look for failure to keep promises; admitted inability to solve problems that the person says are easy. 

Do not expect the Motive Secondary to show powerful action.

Remember that these people are possessed of power but cannot use it to its full development. 

Do not expect the Vital Lacking to be a commercial wizard.

Such a person has cumulative ability, but it will be spasmodic, and subject to periods of desire. 

Remember above all that in the Ideal Balanced Type, neither

Mental, Motive, or Vital characteristics will be so evident as in the single or double types. In the triple combination, no type is strong; but all are harmonious, and hence capable of high development. Where one type is lacking from the Balance, it is apt to be a handicap, lessening the strength of the types which prevail over it. Hence the Balanced Type unless Ideal, or nearly so, is not indicative of a person who has strong characteristics of marked ability. 51

CHARACTER READING BACK HEAD FORMATION 

It would be quite possible to make careful analyses of individuals through study of their heads as viewed from the back. 

There are actual types and combinations of them; but with so many more effective methods of Character Reading, it is hardly advisable to go into minor details.

There are three general shapes of the head as viewed from the back, above the ears. These are: Semicircular, Flat, and Half 

Oval. All heads have tendencies to one of these shapes; the one which the head most nearly resembles should be used as the basis of analysis; and heads which show a half-way tendency should be judged by both. 

Half Oval Semi-Circular Flat Type The Semicircular Head forms a half circle from the tip of one ear to the tip of the other.

It is indicative of impulsiveness; the person with this shape of head acts quickly and does not weigh chances too carefully. 

It is a sign showing freedom from worry over consequences. 

The Flat Head shows dependability; wise choice and deliberation, to a great degree. The person with this shape of head is rather careful in action, and uses a great deal of forethought. 

The Half-Oval Head is not a mixture of balance of the other two types, but a definite separation of the two. Impulsiveness is present as a natural action; but it goes just so far and then stops. 

Caution is aroused at any crucial moment, and the person with this type of head never flies into difficulties that cannot permit retreat without disgrace, or change of policy without creation of suspicion.

Width of the Head as viewed from the back is also indicative of characteristics. Measurements are made visually, by the distance between the ears. 52

Narrowness indicates an easy disposition, a dislike for conflict and an avoidance of quarrel. The individual with this type of head dislikes discourtesy, or embarrassing situations, and tries to overcome them by changing the subject or by settling the dispute in the most available manner. 

Medium Width shows frankness, and thorough desire to face an issue, but with no effort to precipitate a quarrel. The person with the head of medium width will not avoid an important subject even if it is apt to cause difficulty. per- 

Wideness indicates a bluntness that is not offended. The son with this type of head will go out of his way to mention a subject that is bothering him, and will often attempt to make an issue where one does not really exist.

In the above cases, we see distinct separation between Width and Narrowness, with the head of Medium Width part way between the two. Taking the two distinctly opposites: Wide and 

Narrow, it is possible to discover more pronounced characteristics.

The Wide Head shows aggressive energy; a liking for the concrete, material affairs of life; with industry and efficient effort. 

Courage is present, and thorough-going methods are esteemed by people of this type.

Sentiment is lacking, except in a practical way, to persons with Wide Heads. Yet those of this type are not without regard for humanity. They do not betray their emotions, but they are willing to be fair and helpful in their treatment of others. 

The exception comes when thought of other people is classed as sentimental foolishness. Then the person with the Wide Head will be destructive and dangerous, ruled only by a desire for power and possession.

The Narrow Head shows mildness, and willingness to be content with small attainment. Dislike of trouble and difficulty leads people of this type to avoid anything that even suggests conflict, and willingness to take up affairs that are peaceful and gentle.

There is a lack of aggressiveness under new conditions, and those with narrow heads will prefer to go on living under circumstances which offer safety and surety rather than to display their individuality in fields that may bring difficulty.

They are faithful, helpful assistants, willing to work for those who will assume all risks, and generally fearful of being placed in a helpless position. 53

CHARACTER READING 

The one instinct that forces them to abandon this overcaution is that of self-preservation. Most of their lack of ability is imaginary; when they are forced to fight for themselves they will rise to the occasion, purely because it offers the only road to the protection they wish to enjoy.

SIDE HEAD FORMATION Side Head Formation follows logically after Back Head For mation; in fact it is a similar subject, but with a different viewpoint. Here are definite classifications, each having its own story to tell-graphic portrayals of human characteristics. 

The Balanced Head is taken as the type to work from. Theoretically it is an ideal, which shows a proper mixture of definite traits which make up human nature. It is readily recognized by the side view, because its perfect proportions show nothing unusual. It is merely the measuring scale from which other heads, which depart from the Balance, are classified. 

The traits that will be discussed under separate headings are normal qualities existing in the average individual. They are modified in the person who has the Balanced Head, whereas they are manifest to unusual degrees in those whose heads depart from the standard of measurement.

The High Head is greater in height than in length from front to back; that is, when judged by the Balance. A head may be both 

High and Short; or High and Long. In the latter instance it will appear as a Balanced Head, but it will be too large in proportion to the body.

There is a development of faculties in the High Head-people of this type are aspiring and ambitious. Their ideals will be greatly developed, and the natural attributes which they possess will be constantly in evidence. They are benevolent, hopeful, and dignified. They enjoy anything that is idealistic, and they support causes which seem noble and traditional. 

They like education and champion it as the greatest force evolved by mankind. They are visionary, and judge everything by ideals, trying to forget the primitive matters of life. They are particular about formalities, and have great faith in the progress of mankind-whatever their particular plan of progress may be. 

In anything they undertake whether Reason, Will or Desire rules they seek perfection, and dislike inferior motives. 54

5

CHARACTER READING 

They are believers in discipline and strict adherence to systematized methods. Their fault is an over-tendency in this direction; too often they let formalities rule them. They respect titles and dislike to hear people belittle important personages. From this, they may develop too much regard for those who do not deserve it, and too great a contempt for persons whom they consider below their station. They are aristocratic by nature. 

The High Head is easily identified; its unusual proportions lie above the ears more than in the entire height of the head. 

The Low Head is particularly noitceable by its lack of height, particularly above the eyes. In fact, often the eyes seem to be set very close to the forehead. 

From profile to back, such a head shows correct balance; but its lowness is easily recognized. 

This is not a sign of bad traits, but of primitive ideas. Low headed persons are suspicious and mistrustful; they judge life by crude standards, and fail to appreciate complexities of civilization.

They do not lack ideals; but their impression of what is ideal is not lofty. They see nothing in tradition, and do not aspire to great heights, seeking rather to attain practical needs which are within easy reach.

They dislike formalities, and appreciate only those things which are tangible and definite. Theories, abstract ideas, and poetic thoughts mean nothing to them.

They are apt to be superstitious and they are greatly pleased with small accomplishments. They have pride in achievements, but they regard as achievements things which the High Head person would ignore. Titles do not impress them unless they can gain them for themselves; having done so, they are easily satisfied. As a burgess of a small community, the Low Headed man will consider himself as in a class with the President of the United States.

They can be pushed and urged to new achievement, but they are so easily satisfied that they normally prefer to hold on to whatever they have gained and be suited with it. They are clever and very often ingenious in their methods, but they do not have vision, and will frequently fail when they go outside their field. 

They enjoy public life; are fond of applause, and like to occupy the lime-light. They enter contests purely with the purpose of winning, and are tenacious and capable of the utmost 56

effort. They believe that the winner should get all, and the loser nothing.

The Long Head has over-developed proportions from the profile to the back of the head.

Such persons are planners. To them, the future is far more important than the present. They will deny themselves pleasure and immediate return purely because they see some greater benefit coming later.

Hence they are often classed as unselfish; but that is hardly true, for these people think of themselves-but of themselves as they will be, and not as they are now. 

They are emotional rather than intellectual, and they make friends very easily. They keep their friendships and they know how to understand people. Thus they believe in cooperation and fraternity, largely because they see that such practices may be of great development in the future.

They have pleasant dispositions, and are good students, because they know that accumulated knowledge is to their benefit. 

They like to have a purpose in life; to choose an occupation which promises advancement and prestige so that years from now they can look back and admire their old foresight. 

In the study of Long Heads, two distinct types are noted: (a) Those which are long from forehead to ears as well as from ears to back of head; (b) Those which are long from ears to back of head only.

The first class (a) shows high development of the senses; retentive memory, keen perception, and other mental qualities that are above the average. Such persons are calculators, linguists, and powerful thinkers along practical lines. They can be said to have foresight in a practical way, taking up new things, remembering all they hear, gaining versatility and talent because of its future value.

The second class (b) are those who do not have such development of perception and retentiveness. They do not show versatility and readiness to absorb ideas; they may be forgetful. But they have the same purpose-foresight; and they use it through friendship, family relationships, and social life. 

They mingle with people and enter into humanitarian activities; they are fond of animals, and are of kindly disposition. 

They are sympathetic and have social inclinations. 

The Long Head (a) with frontal development is more typically masculine, and is most often observed in men. 

The 

58

Long Head (b) with back head development is more typically feminine and is most often observed in women. 

The Short Head is about the same in length as in width. It displays very little distance behind the ears.

The person who has this type of head is not noted for foresight. He or she is governed by the present, concentrating all effort on the task in hand, with no concern about what may follow.

These people do not make friends easily or seek variety in social affairs. They are not particularly sympathetic, and pay a great deal of attention to their own affairs. 

They are apt to be grasping, seeking to get immediate return for their efforts, rather than waiting for the development of the future. They do not consider a matter until the time arrives for it, and they are fond of waiting until the last minute. 

They are lacking in sales ability, due to the absence of forethought and sociability, for they are apt to be inconsiderate and forgetful of promises.

But they succeed in impersonal matters; they can manage their own affairs, and work by themselves. 

They like to start things, feeling that they will be capable of keeping them going; but they do not make extensive plans. 

They are governed by the theory that anything in operation is useful and valuable, and that strict attention to it will open up development. This is a contrast to the Long Head who plans far in advance.

The Balanced Head 

With an understanding of the characteristics of the different types of heads, we can analyze the Balance Head. This is the 

Ideal Type that values tradition, and upholds intellectuality, yet does not give them more than they are worth.

Ready to throw aside formality, and judge matters by their face value, the person with the Balanced Head will find a temporary satisfaction in small achievement, yet will regard it purely as a stepping stone to high achievement.

Normally perceptive and retentive, people with Balanced Heads will be friendly, and thoughtful of the future; will be good mixers, but will choose their acquaintances carefully. 

They will devote considerable thought to the present, but will not be satisfied unless their plans show them some future possibility. 59

CHARACTER READING Composite Head Types Obviously the Long Head and the Short Head cannot make a combination; nor can the High Head and the Low Head, for a composition of any of these pairs will bring a Balance. 

But a head can be High and Long; High and Short; Low and Long; Low and Short. While these show combined qualities, they can be summarized by short paragraphs which explain the mingling of traits.

High and Long Head 

This is the person who looks far into the future, with great ambitions and high ideals, particularly in the field of intellectual accomplishment. Precise ideas, great adherence to form and exactness are evident, with the desire to make friends among important people. Such a person will have high accomplishments and attainments, and will be aristocratic by nature; yet will judge aristocracy as a matter of talent and not of birth. 

With the Long Head developed at the back, family pride will be more evident, and these persons will be of the type who talk of their ancestors; yet feel that they must live up to the past, and make the future even more notable.

High and Short 

There is a variance in persons of this type. Some of them become very scholarly and mind their own affairs, so that few people understand them. They live individual lives, like crabs in a shell; and are neither appreciated nor disliked, although they may be admired for their abilities.

The other class which is most pronounced are those who set high ideals and apply them to themselves, feeling that they have some attribute of birth, or attainment that makes them superior. 

They are very particular, and will only choose occupations or environments that carry prestige; but they will pride themselves on their intellects, and will believe that they are extremely important. They are lacking in sympathy and emotion, and will often set their own standards whether they are ethical or not. 

Low and Long

This is the type of person who is rather primitive and rough, caring little for formality, yet sympathetic and judging persons by merit rather than position. 60

With Long Frontal, they will be quick, perceptive and keen, ready in wit and clever in action, yet not intellectual.

With Long Back Head, they will be friendly with everyone, giving good practical advice for the future, and showing sympathy, although they themselves may never rise to individual attainment. 

Low and Short

This type of person is primitive and selfish, trying to gain everything quickly, and paying no attention to the future. They are easily pleased with anything that is thrown their way; they will be proud of momentary importance and will seek it constantly.

The typical Low Head qualities are found in them, and are accentuated because of lack of emotion and forethought. Where the Low Head person tries to keep what he gets, the Low Short Head individual does not think further than the act of obtaining. 

Summary of Side Head Formation 

The High Head is intellectual and idealistic. 

The Low Head is primitive and crude. 

The Long Head has forethought, and human understanding. 

The Short Head thinks of the present. 

Other Head Formations 

The Proceeding Forehead indicates thoughtfulness and meditation. It is a characteristic of the philosophical mind. People of this type are meditative and reflective; they are painstaking and deliberate, and do not end their effort until they have made attainment. 

They may think slowly, but they think carefully. They do not talk much, but they speak wisely. Their opinions are valuable; but they are impractical in material affairs, although their sense of reasoning is exceptional. They are imaginative and original in their theories. The Proceeding Forehead is more noticeably of the Feminine Type. 

The Receding Forehead is a sign of activity and impulsiveness, with energetic effort present. People of this type put theories into practice, but they do not go deeply into a study of such matters. 

They are easily bored with long explanations. As readers they skip pages, seeking only the meat of a story and having no 61

CHARACTER READING 

great appreciation of dramatic, well written scenes. They are keen in observation, but do not analyze. 

Impatience is a common quality of the Receding Forehead. 

Persons of this type do not understand why certain things are appreciated; they have no finesse, and lack order and system. 

PROFILE FORMATION 

Profiles are of three types: Convex, Concave, and Plane. 

It is easy enough to visualize the extremes of these types. 

CONVEX PROFILE 

All features Convex. Refer to this illustration for each detail.

The Convex Profile 

Starting from the top of the forehead, and going to beneath the chin, the pure Convex Profile fits into a circle. The downward, outward slope of the forehead; the extended nose, protruding lips and receding chin all enter the picture. 

The general qualities of the Convex Profile are sharpness, shrewdness, and penetration. People of this type are eager and keen, always alert, and finding interest in everything. 62

CONCAVE PROFILE 

All features Concave. Refer to this illustration for each detail.

The Concave Profile 

This is as nearly opposite the Convex as possible. The forehead is extended, the nose is short and set back; the lips are not prominent, and the chin protrudes. 

Here the circle goes inward, striking the base of the nose and not the tip.

The general qualities of the Concave Profile are passiveness and slowness. People of this type ponder over matters, and work out patient plans. They are rather absent-minded and content with their own thoughts.

They mind their own affairs and are not alert, looking for everything. On the contrary they recede, like their features, and contemplate those things which have made an impression upon them. 63

CHARACTER READING PLANE PROFILE 

All features Plane. Refer to this illustration for each detail.

The Plane Profile 

The true Plane Profile forms a straight vertical line from the chin to the forehead, this line passing through the base of the nose.

Firmness is a distinctive trait of this type. People of Plane 

Profile have definite ideas and courses of action. They are deliberate and thoughtful, but they have no hesitancy in their methods. They plan and execute, extending their own affairs to influence others, but restricting themselves to their own fields of effort. They think to a purpose. 

Minglings of Profiles

The brief summary of Profile traits recorded above is based on the fact that all the features: forehead, eye, nose, mouth and chin are either Convex, Concave or Plane. 64

Therefore the Convex Profile, so briefly analyzed, is a formation of Convex Forehead, Convex Eye, Convex Nose, Convex 

Mouth, and Convex Chin.

Each of these features has its own significance; hence the natural way in which to study Profile is not by a composite summary, but by an analysis of each individual feature. This, in pure types, will automatically produce a summary of Convex, Concave, or Plane; but it will also enable the student to understand Profiles that have different types of features. 

The Forehead 

The forehead is indicative of mental processes. The observational faculties of the brain are located in the lower part of the forehead, while the faculties of reason and reflection are situated higher.

The Convex Forehead has its development at the lower part; hence the individual with this type of forehead is particularly observant, and of keen vision. He sees everything; depends upon his eyes to tell him what is correct. "Seeing is believing" is his motto.

Persons with this type of forehead are interested in facts and are impressed by them. They are not content with descriptions; they want to see things for themselves. They are students of facial expression; they know when something important is about to happen, and they watch everything. 

They are pointed in their remarks, and to them many matters are plainly evident. They read quickly and rapidly, and see things which escape the attention of other people. 

The Concave Forehead indicates the listener. It is the sign of the theorist, who wants a reason for everything and whose mind develops a variety of thoughts from one suggestion. 

People with this type of forehead are not satisfied with mere facts; they wish to go further and to learn if things are theoretically sound. They will often ignore facts that are plainly evident, purely because, to their way of reasoning, the facts should be incorrect.

They can reason themselves into believing that certain things are true, even when the visual evidence points to the contrary. 

They are impressed by the testimony of other people, whether verbal or written.

They dislike to make quick decisions, and like to think while they work; hence when suited for physical labor, they can best perform routine jobs where close attention is not necessary. 65

CHARACTER READING 

The Plane Forehead, a vertical line, shows both perceptive and reflective faculties. Persons with this type of forehead are good observers, but they think over everything that they see, and give it consideration.

They keep their minds on their work, and will give great attention to small details; they are not subject to every impression, trying to think rapidly all the while as are persons with Convex Foreheads, nor are they meditative and wandering like the Concave Forehead type.

They have the ability to concentrate their attention carefully on a subject and to take a reasonable view all along without either forming quick conclusions or indulging in long theories that carry them away from the actual facts of the matter. 

The Eye 

The formation of the eye, in relation to the Profile gives an inkling into the subject of speech and conversation. 

The cheek, and not the forehead, is the basis of judgment for the formation of the eye, from the Profile view. The eye must be considered individually, without comparison with the brow, which belongs to the forehead. 

The Convex Eye extends well in advance of the cheek-bone. 

A vertical line, passing upward from the cheek will come in back of the pupil of the eye.

The person with the Convex Eye is a rapid talker, having the ability to discourse fluently and extemporaneously. Such people frequently get ahead of themselves, and try to express too many thoughts at once. They are verbose, explaining matters in too many ways, and repeating themselves often. They are not discriminating in choice of words, and when they wax loquacious they leave very little opportunity for other persons to speak. 

They catch little points and make issues of them; they argue trivialities of a subject; they have a reply for everything; but their efforts are not over-convincing, and they are frequently forced to change their arguments.

They are frank and candid in their speech, because they go into subjects so fully that they leave very little to conceal. They are good at descriptions, because they mention so many details, but they have too many side-issues to discourse deeply upon a single important matter. 66

The Concave Eye denotes the person who is deliberate in speech and who frequently says nothing because he does not know how to express his thoughts.

Such people sometimes drawl when they talk, and they generally show lack of conversational ability. 

In answering questions they do not counter, but usually say "Yes" or "No"- —or even resort to a nod or a shake of the head.

They are confused by rapid talk and will often require a second explanation when they are listening to a speedy speaker. 

Yet they are not wanting in the power of expression. With a few well-chosen words they can tell their story. They will listen to a long harangue and will then summarize it in a few short sentences.

When they have the opportunity to speak deliberately, their utterances will be excellent, and their expressions forceful. They give power to words, not through eloquence or by brilliant metaphors, but by plain straightforward expressions. 

They often keep their thoughts to themselves, having learned by experience that words unsaid do not have to be retracted. 

The Concave Eye is situated behind the vertical line drawn upward from the cheek-bone. The pupil is noticeably to the rear of this line.

The Plane Eye denotes the type of person who has much to say but who waits to the proper time to say it. Such people are good listeners and excellent speakers.

They do not discourse freely on subjects with which they have only a surface knowledge; but they talk freely on anything with which they are familiar, and when they begin to express themselves, the surety and comparative ease of their remarks crowds other opinions into the background. 

They are not short of words; they have good vocabularies, and develop a knowledge of the use of language. Hence the thoughts that they express are clearly understood and are applauded.

While they can talk for a long while, they are never rapid in speech. They are steady and easy in method, seldom at a loss. 

They are regarded as the best of talkers because of their wisdom and choice in matters of conversation. 

The Plane Eye has a pupil which is just about on a vertical line drawn upward from the cheek-bone. 67

CHARACTER READING The Nose 

The nose is a sign of initiative. It symbolizes a natural energy, which can be attributed partly to ease of breathing. The person with the large nose notices disagreeable odors and is responsive to fragrant atmosphere more than the person who has a small nose.

The Convex Nose is the so-called Roman nose. It indicates a person with vigor, and a desire to obtain results in any undertaking.

When people with the Convex Nose discuss a subject, they wish to do so rapidly and to a purpose. They do not like to leave things until a later time. They dislike long conferences, and extended business meetings. 

They are not annoyed by discussions if everything said has a point. But they want to come to real issues, and to decide them. They like to say "Yes" or "No" if something pleases them or does not meet with their approval; and when they make a proposal they expect the same sort of a reply. 

The Convex Nose is all extended; it may not be overly large, but it does not recede from the base. The bridge is apt to be high; if so, it denotes further vigor. 

The Concave Nose is short and stubby, with a slight upward tilt. It is usually small, and sometimes seems almost flat against the face. It seems to have a tendency to recede as much as possible.

It is a sign of an easy disposition, with a tendency toward secret thought.

The person with the Concave Nose is often fond of wasting time. Such persons plod along and keep at work, but do not accomplish things rapidly, and are willing to put off matters indefinitely.

In contrast to the Convex Nose type they will drop important subjects in order to amuse themselves or discuss trivialities, whereas the person with the Convex Nose is always vigorous, even when off duty.

People of the Concave Nose group delight in long and frequent conferences where no decision is reached. They think that à conference itself is an affair of accomplishment, and an additional one will be further work. 

They do not judge by results, but by continual activity; they are tortoise-like in their ways, ready to take their time, con- 68

fident that they will arrive-no matter how long the period of the journey may be.

They are experts at passing the time away, in a restful, easygoing manner. They can be comfortable in stuffy rooms, and are not greatly impressed by pleasing atmosphere.

The Plane Nose is neither long nor short; it has no protruding qualities, nor does it recede and it does not show a high bridge nor a deep inward curve between the eyes.

It is a well-formed nose-a nose that does not show as a startling or definite part of the profile either by conspicuousness or by stubbiness.

The person with the Plane Nose is industrious but not vigorous. Such people take conferences as a matter of routine, and are not annoyed if matters are not settled; but they do not like to be disturbed by frequent discussions of this sort. 

They present propositions and expect them to be acted upon within a reasonable length of time. They will bring up forgotten subjects at intervals, more as a reminder than as a demand for action.

They do not make quick affirmative or negative decisions, but require time to consider a matter. When they are approached again on such questions, they do not object, but will finally come to a definite decision. 

They like to lay work aside and indulge in occasional pleasures, but if something very important is on hand, they will not neglect it. They seek results, but not quickly. They accomplish but expect time.

Disposition The Mouth or indisposition-is indicated by the mouth.

Both the lips and the teeth enter into the consideration of the different types of mouths.

The Convex Mouth has heavy, protruding lips, and large front teeth are usual in this type.

People with the Convex Mouth are frequently irritable and quick to criticise. When they are troubled by indigestion or slight illness, they become ill-tempered and subject to grouchiness.

They will make angry remarks and will exaggerate them, not meaning what they say, but trying to momentarily express the wrath which is within them. Such remarks must not be 69

CHARACTER READING 

harshly judged for they are not intentional, and do not express the true feelings of the individual. 

They have a way of making themselves appear unpleasant when they feel unpleasant and this leads them to be misunderstood. They quibble about little matters, even when they are feeling well; talk about "bad luck," and "tough breaks"; yet all this is merely on the surface. 

As the Convex Profile fits into a circle, a noticeable feature of the mouth of the Convex type is the fact that the upper lip is at the bottom of a slightly inward slant from the nose, while the lower lip is full, protruding, with an inward curve below. 

The Concave Mouth comes back behind the base of the nose; it has small, thin lips, and a forward curve from the bottom of the lower lip. The teeth are usually small. 

A person with this type of mouth has a pleasant way of speaking; an easy manner, and no inclination to be grouchy. If such people do not feel well, they do not take it out on others. 

They have a pleasant matter-of-fact tactfulness, and none of the sarcasm which is often evident in persons with the Concave Mouth. 

The Plane Mouth is straight and firm, with lips of moderate size. The upper lip comes from a vertical line beneath the nose, and there is no marked inward or outward trend immediately below the lower lip.

This is an expressive type of mouth, for it does not show displeasure except when occasion demands. Where the person with the Concave Mouth misses opportunities where righteous wrath may be displayed, the man or woman with the Plane 

Mouth will express well-founded disapproval. 

Those who have Plane Mouths are sometimes fault-finders, but they have rather an agreeable way and their sarcasm is mild and never unfair.

The Chin 

We learn something of physical deportment from an analysis of the chin. It is the final sign of the Profile, and is an index of patience or lack of it.

The Convex Chin is the receding chin-the chin which may be almost no chin at all. It denotes impatience, and the desire to avoid delay.

Those who have the Convex Chin are hurried in action, and are not sustained in effort. They arrive unexpectedly, and leave 70

quickly. In trivial matters they are sudden and spontaneous. 

Their feelings are quickly aroused; they become indignant and calm down a moment later. They speak emphatically, urged by the thought of the moment, and then lapse into indifference, or change the subject entirely.

The Concave Chin is a very noticeable part of the profile. 

It extends beyond the base of the nose, and is large, rounded and lengthy when seen in its extreme development. 

It shows deliberate physical activity, with purpose; slowness but with no tendency to brook interference. 

People with Concave Chins are somewhat obstinate. They do not flare up or become annoyed by petty matters, but when they have submitted to considerable discomfort or inconvenience, they show their anger suddenly and emphatically. 

The Plane Chin strikes the line that runs vertically through the base of the nose. It is often quite straight, but may be rounded, and is usually so with persons who have a composite Profile.

The persons with the Plane Chin are impatient with some things, and patient with others. When they can see nothing to be gained by becoming impatient, they do not do so. They do not mind waiting in line, or long train rides if these things are the only way out; but they do object if they have to wait for someone who has neglected to keep an appointment on time. 

This is in contrast to the Convex Chin which will fret at any inconvenience, and the Concave Chin which will wait all day without minding it, no matter what the cause may be. 

Those of Plane Chins display rare symptoms of nervous energy. They keep their patience for a reasonable time; then act like the impatient Convex Chin type, but with more emphasis. They never display the long endurance seen in the Concave Chin persons. Their physical actions are governed more by reason than by the instinctive inclinations seen in the other types.

Feature Lines 

Quite apart from the fundamental Profiles of Convex, Concave and Plane formation are the tendencies of Feature Lines.

These have a subordinate importance, but they are much in evidence with some persons.

The average person has level Feature Lines, when considered from the horizontal view; but there are certain types of per- 71

CHARACTER READING 

sons-easily recognized-whose features depart definitely from the level.

Inasmuch as these involve the Full Face as well as the Profile view, they will be discussed in the next section, as the first link between Formation and Physiognomy; and they should be studied as a direct continuation of remarks on the Profile. 

PHYSIOGNOMY 

When the police wish to capture a criminal, they broadcast two pictures of him: one, a full-face view; the other a profile. 

These make a definite impression upon the people who see them, and through this means many a dangerous character has been brought to justice.

It is not just the features that serve as a guide; but the characteristics that are portrayed there. The indications of the photograph are instinctively recognized by many people, even though they have no systematic way of classifying the information which they have gained. 

One can tell much from a photograph. The Character 

Reader does not actually have to see a person to make an anaysis; good, clear photographs are of value. This method is used by employers who have adopted Character Reading as a definite way of judging men who seek work. 

From these two views: Profile and Full Face, formation is easily studied. The Back Head, while it has been considered, is not essential.

Studying the Profile, the_reader will quickly realize that Formation is the key-note. But so far, we have merely considered the outline in the Full Front view. 

Why? Because the frontal features are a new subject. They are the basis of Physiognomy, the art of reading Character by the lineaments of the face.

We approached Physiognomy when we studied Profile Formation; for there we were departing from general form of the head, and were considering the individual portions of the face.

Now, beginning with the general characteristics of Feature lines, which include both Profile and Full Face, the reader will proceed to the individual features as revealed through Physiognomy. 72

Feature Lines 

The level Feature Lines denote the average person; yet in some faces, a distinct departure is evident. To a lesser degree, certain resemblances are seen in most faces. 

So in Upward and Downward Features, we have the extremes; and it is evident that the characteristics they portray will be present to a modified degree in those whose features show slight traces of one of these two qualities. 

Upward Features are present somewhat in actual formation of the Profile. But more than that they are seen in the lips, the curve of the eyebrows, and in the corners of the eyes.

Upwardness shows a happy optimism. This is present in the individual features which will be discussed later. Upwardness shows a tendency to contentment, a good disposition, and an ease of action. • Downward Features seen both in the Profile formation and in the mobile parts of the face, point toward pessimism, and discontentment. 

Downwardness is indicative of a mistrustful, unhappy disposition, inclined to seriousness and forgetful of jollity. 

Rounded, Curving Features are typical of the Feminine Type and they may be either Upward or Downward-sometimes Level.

Their tendency is usually Upward, with an indication of gentleness and tolerance. 

Straight, Angular Features are of the more Masculine Type, and are also either Upward, Downward, or Level, but with a more frequent Downward tendency, signifying a stern, unyielding disposition.

The Forehead 

There are many classifications of the Forehead, from the standpoint of Physiognomy. It is a mistake to group these under numerous headings, for they have been partially treated under Formation; and there is a distinct difference between that subject and Physiognomy. Those types which are listed below are all apedially evident in a person's appearance. 

The Short Forehead does not extend high above the eyebrows. It is met by the line of the hair, so that a very short space lies between.

It is a sign of directness, bluntness of action, and definite expression. A person with this type of forehead says what he 73

CHARACTER READING 

means and means what he says. It portrays the straightforward thinker rather than the deep thinker.

The Prominent Forehead shows two distinct types: (a) Broad, prominent, with high line of hair, but with greater width above the eyebrows than at the hair-line. 

This indicates the calculative thinker; the man who has keen perception and uses it in scientific and orderly ways. Ability to solve problems quickly, and to systematize affairs is present in this type. (b) Broad, prominent, with high line of hair, but with greater width at the hair-line than above the eyebrows. 

This is the philosophical type of forehead. It tells us that the persons studies life, has a good memory, and retentive faculties, with humorous appeal.

A forehead with slight bulges accentuates this type. 

The Developed Forehead shows prominence, width, and height to the hair-line but its lower and upper proportions are almost equal.

This indicates the person of the inventive type; the possible genius, who can analyze and make abstract matters tangible. 

The High Narrow Forehead, with rather prominent temples is a sign of the scientific mind. It shows the person who is primarily an investigator; who desires to look into matters and learn what they are about. It is a practical sort of type, concentrating upon a single subject.

The Small, Narrow Forehead with depressed temples, shows smallness of ideas and narrowness of views. People with this type of forehead are usually concerned with single purposes which are not of great moment. Their opinions are restricted, and they lack the ability to gain widespread appeal. 

The Extended Forehead is a type which does not have a high hair-line at the center, but shows extensions at the sides-above the temples, where the hair recedes.

This is a diplomatic type; it indicates people who are direct only when occasions demands, and who have an easy way of gaining the confidence of other people.

The Wrinkled Forehead shows concentration; the desire to employ great effort and to take matters seriously. It is a sign of a constructive thinker; one who is never sparing in desire to gain purpose and results, and who will not overlook details. 74

The Heavy Forehead is firm and bony over the eyes; smooth, but with a downward pressure that causes lines between the eyes.

It indicates intensity of thought; mental energy, and firm opinions; confidence in important matters, and slowness in change of opinion.

Summary: The indications described may appear in combination. If none of them are present to any noticeable degree, the individual does not possess the marked qualities which these distinctive types portray.

The Eyebrows 

Eyebrows form various divisions, and it is best to analyze them separately, combining the tendencies which are indicated in order to form a good analysis. 

Growth, Hair, and Shape are the main divisions.

Growth of Eyebrows refers to the amount of hair-not to its appearance or its texture.

Heavy Growth shows eyebrows with a great deal of hair; they are wide and thick. This is a sign of intensity and strength of Will, Reason or Desire, whichever of those three qualities may be evident in the individual. They portray a Masculine

Type. 

Moderate Growth is typical of many eyebrows; they are not an outstanding feature of the face from the viewpoint of prevalence. They indicate a rather changeable temperament, with a combination of mental concentration and application. 

Light Growth means the thin, almost pencilled Eyebrow, that is a very fine, slender line. They indicate delicacy of thought and temperament, and signify the Feminine Type. 

Eyebrows that are Close Together, and almost join, are usually of the heavy type, and they show the type of individual who is extreme in intensity, inclined to sensuality, and strong in emotional feelings. It is a nervous sign. 

Eyebrows that are Wide Apart with an unusual distance between them show the type of person who lacks strong convictions; who is not intense, but easily surprised and quite credulous. 

The Hair of the Eyebrows is either Bushy and Coarse, or 

Fine and Smooth. 75

CHARACTER READING 

Bushy, Coarse Hair shows certain traits of genius, but an irritability of disposition, and a tendency to be eccentric. 

Smooth, Fine Hair tells of an evenness of thought; the ability to avoid discouragement, and set, well-founded purpose. 

The Shape of the Eyebrows 

Here are definite divisions; quick, effective ways of understanding some of the hidden characteristics of people that could be learned in no other way. Arched Downward

EYEBROWS 

Curving Straight Upward

Straight Eyebrows show activity, and the people who have them are wide awake and alert. These Eyebrows follow a line that is nearly horizontal, allowing for only a very slight curve. 

They show practical thinking, mental stability, and matter-offact ideas. 76

Downward Eyebrows straight or curved, but with a noticeable downward slope, are typical of the impatient individual. 

A distinct droop shows melancholy attributes-a mind that yields to grief and despair.

Where the curve appears noticeably between the eyes, the brows thinning out at the sides, artfulness is present, with the undesirable quality of treachery. 

Upward Eyebrows show the argumentative, ambitious type of person, who is frequently impatient. When they stop abruptly, they indicate an optimistic trend of mind; yet they do not give signs of practicability.

Curving Eyebrows that form a sinuous double curve, generally upward at the center and downward at the extremes, indicate the designing type of person. Untrustworthiness, jealousy and temper are often traits of persons who have such Eyebrows. 

Arched Eyebrows present a variety of types. If their angle is abrupt, they indicate imaginative qualities; thoughts that are high and pleasant, but subject to sudden change. 

Abrupt decisions are made by people who have these Eyebrows, and they sway constantly from ecstacy to moodiness. 

Dreaminess and belief in implausible ideas are very frequent in eyebrows of this class.

High Set Eyebrows (which are frequently of the Arched Type) denote the inquiring mind which is often somewhat credulous and easily deceived. 

Low Set Eyebrows that overhang the eyes denote discernment with an ability to analyze and meditate carefully upon important subjects.

The Eyes 

The expression of the Eyes is one of the most valuable methods of determining Character. Writers speak of "bright eyes,' "wistful eyes," "melancholy eyes" because they know that such terms convey an immediate impression to those who read them. 

Eyes give us close contact with personalities. When two persons gaze at one another, they come to a closer understanding. 

The surest way for a person of intelligence to quiz the sincerity of another human being is to look him squarely in the eye. The person of lesser mentality will then find it dicult to be deceiving.

That is good theory, but practically it does not work all the time, because many people have learned to guard themselves 77

CHARACTER READING 

against it. Some have learned to meet a questioning glance innocently and calmly; while others deliberately affect an expression to give a wrong idea of their thoughts. 

The shifting eye that seeks to avoid glances is usually the most easily detected, because that is the primitive way of the person who has not become accomplished in deception; yet just because an individual is evasive in his glance, one should not convict him of insincerity. He may be shy or timid.

Nevertheless the eyes are a focal spot of Character Reading. 

The analyst, however, does not go by unreliable indications. 

He studies not only the eyes themselves, but their formation and their position, and their relationship to the eye-lids as well. 

General Characteristics of Eyes 

Bright, Sparkling Eyes show a keenness of observation with quick understanding. They denote a rather cheery nature. They also show a slight tendency toward emotional traits. 

Dull Eyes indicate an introspective nature with an inclination to listlessness. They are contemplative and a trifle melancholy.

Formation of Eyes ROUND EYES 

Eyes are elliptical in shape, but some have a tendency to the circular form. These are called Round Eyes. 

Such eyes show confidence, and trustfulness in human nature.

Persons who have them view life from the good side, and do not understand its worst elements.

They think the best of everyone, and overlook faults as a rule, preferring to set up a standard of kindliness without doubting that others will live up to it.

They rely upon the better nature of people and cannot understand treachery. If conditions appear good, they are satisfied, and will express disbelief in rumors to the contrary.

ANGULAR EYES 

Eyes that are Angular are more oval-shaped than Round Eyes, yet they are symmetrical, and a careful examination will show that they are almost straight at spots along the lids. 

Persons with Angular Eyes are whimsical, and indulge in fancies. They are rather cynical in their outlook on life and do not hesitate to bring up doubts as to the sincerity or scrupulousness of persons whom they are discussing. 78

They often exaggerate this view-point, making statements which they do not entirely believe; but they like to make extravagant sayings, and are fond of anything that may indicate scandal. They like rumors and help to spread them. But they are not mean or unfair in disposition. Their outlook is no more inexact than that of the type previously discussed. 

CURVED EYES 

These are the Almond-Shaped Eyes, that have curved upper lids and rather straight lower lids; that are narrow at the outside corners. They bear a resemblance to the eyes of the Japanese and Chinese, although they are seldom so extreme. 

People who have Curved Eyes are secretive and keep much knowledge to themselves. They are not always purposeful in their deception, but believe that their own interests are often benefitted by not telling people of their affairs. 

They do not tell facts or rumors regarding other persons; hence they are good people in whom to confide. When they become confidential themselves, they make it evident that they are departing from their usual rule. 

They are of rather quiet disposition, and always pretend to know less than they actually do. This trait is particularly evident in children, who frequently surprise people by mentioning things that have been forgotten.

Positions of Eyes PROMINENT EYES 

These are eyes which are forward, sometimes almost bulging. They are not necessarily Convex (from Profile Formation) but they are very often of that type, and carry similar traits. 

They indicate the talkative person, who can relate anecdotes and supply many of them. They indicate a quality of action and interest in life and amusements, with lack of moodiness. 

Eyes that are very bulging indicate cruel ambition that will stop at nothing.

MEDIUM EYES 

These are normal eyes, that are neither Prominent nor Deep Set. They are often seen with the Plane Profile, but they may be either Convex or Concave.

The person with such eyes is a ready listener, but usually on the point of talking. Such people are quietly observant, and 79.

CHARACTER READING 

inclined to discuss both sides of a question. They become serious at times, but can usually find some cure for worry. 

DEEP-SET EYES 

The Deep-Set Eyes seem sunk in the head, and look as though they were trying to escape detection. They frequently appear with the Concave Profile.

Such eyes show a melancholy disposition, and are often inclined to be mystical. They have a habit of looking into space, and show pensiveness and thought. 

They are the eyes of the recluse who is ready to abandon the world; to retire into his own thoughts and forget everything else.

UPWARD AND DOWNWARD TENDENCIES 

The student should remember that Upward and Downward tendencies of Horizontal Feature Lines are very evident in the Eyes. The term "Laughing Eyes" is not a misnomer; and "Sad Eyes" are a fact also.

The indications of such qualities are seen in the outer corners of the eyes, where wrinkles appear, especially when the eyes are partly closed. Sometimes when a person smiles his eyes show sadness; and this is a sure sign that his disposition is rather serious and inclined to gloom. 

Eyes that really appear to laugh are always a sure sign of the person who is happy at heart, and whose disposition is always on the verge of merriment.

Eye Distances 

This is an important factor in the analysis of Eyes. The 

Medium distance between eyes is a trifle less than the width of the eye itself. A distance greater than this indicates Wideness; whereas a lesser distance is a sign of Narrowness. 

Wideness Between the Eyes

This is a sign of poor judgment-an aptitude for talking well of people who do not deserve it. It is also indicative of poor balance, and although it shows broad-mindedness, people who have this unusual width are very easily tempted and led to believe that wrong is right. They will become dishonest on occasions when they see great profit possible, losing their sense of restraint because of temporary excitement. 80

Normal Width 

This is a sign of good judgment, common sense and carefulness. People who have Normal Width are rather broadminded and fair, but they are quick to detect those who seem to deceive them. They are not easily tempted and are immune from schemers, as a rule. 

Narrowness Between the Eyes 

This shows a suspicious temperament. People of this type deal in little matters, and narrow vision. They are apt to be unscrupulous about matters of minor consequence, but free from temptation where important things are involved. 

They sometimes give way to desire to appropriate things which do not belong to them; but they do so in a petty way, feeling security in the fact that it is a small matter. They can be trusted with large amounts, because they are somewhat overawed, and feel a sudden sense of responsibility. 

They are ordinarily honest, but cannot always resist the lure of pettiness. They are watchful and rather sly in action.

The Eyes and the Eyelids The Eyelids play a part in the Physiognomy of the Eye purely because they do or do not obscure part of the eye from view. This gives three general classes of Eyes, as judged from the action of the Eyelids.

The Wide-Open Eye 

The Wide-Open Eye is a sign of credulity and wonderment. 

It is questioning and ready to believe. It is recognized by the relation of the eyelids to the iris; the iris is often entirely visible and the white of the eye is sometimes seen below it and above it. Even if the iris is not entirely visible, the Eye may show 

Wide-Open qualities. 

People with such Eyes are passive rather than creative; they accept the opinions of other persons without forming any of their own; and they seem to be constantly thirsting for information which they do not use after they have it. If their eyes have a far-away expression, or a peculiar stare, they are apt to be gullible to the extreme. 

Eyes With High Pupils and Iris

Eyes of this type can be classed also as those with low Upper Lids. They give the effect of the Pupils peering out from be- 81

CHARACTER READING 

neath the Lids, with white showing between the bottom of the 

Iris and the Lower Lids. 

Such Eyes show an intense nature; they are Eyes of passion and envy; vindictive traits; shrewdness and self-centered impulses.

If the Eyes slant inwards, it is a sign of treachery, and a dangerous, sometimes destructive nature.

EYES 

Low Iris

Close Lids Wide Open 

High Iris 

High Iris and Pupils 

Eyes With High Iris

In these Eyes, the Pupils are not obscured by the Upper Lids; but the white appears between the bottom of the Iris and the Lower Lids. They are not so noticeably high as the previous type. 82

They are Eyes that show clear thought, and capability that goes with it; but they also signify the sensuous nature and explain why persons yield to dissipation.

Such Eyes do not appear to be hiding under the Upper Lids, but their position seems definitely high. Use the Pupil as an important base of judgment. It should not do more than touch the Upper Lid. 

Eyes With Low Iris

These Eyes have coloring matter which is partly obscured by the Lower Lids, but with white showing above the Iris. 

Sometimes the Pupils almost go below the level of the Lower 

Lids. 

They point to a cruel, heartless disposition; a type of person who is not treacherous, but who is often openly antagonistic, and who does not mind suffering.

Eyes With Both Lids Close 

These Eyes are immediately recognized. The Upper Lids obscure part of the Iris, and the Lower Lids do likewise. Sometimes the Eyes are a trifle high-set; that is the Lower Lids barely obscure the Iris.

Such Eyes indicate concentration-both mental and physical. 

They portray the type of person who is exact in observation, who can recognize differences in things which are very much alike.

Those Eyes which are a trifle high in this class show preference for mental effort; while the Eyes that seem a bit lower are indicative of the individual who judges and concentrates upon practical, physical matters. 

It is the Lids which form such Eyes and when the Eyes are of the Round type, the Lids will be very prominent. 

Other Features of Eyes 

Very Narrow Eyes; that seem to be purposely kept closed, and which are almost squinting, denote the skeptical mind, and when they form a very narrow slit, it is a sign of a sly, distrustful nature.

Large and Small Iris: The Large Iris shows a warmhearted inclination, no matter what the particular nature of the person may be. This is, of course, modified or accentuated according to the more important traits revealed by the Eyes. 83

CHARACTER READING 

The Small Iris, on the contrary, indicates coldness and lack of quick enthusiasm. It is typical of the Eye which shows full development of the cruel nature. 

Large and Small Pupils: The Pupil of course, is subject to dilation; but unless the light is very bright, it will assume its normal size, and can be classed as large or small. 

The Large Pupil indicates a confiding nature-a person who is generous with his thoughts, observing the affairs of others and applying interest to them.

The Small Pupil indicates thoughts that pertain to self; the person whose disposition is to consider personal interest as a basis, and to withhold confidence. 

Lines Between the Eyes 

Lines between the Eyes are often caused by the Heavy Forehead, although they may be evident in other types. The prevalence of such lines gives definite indications. 

A Single Line-usually deep and long and vertical-denotes unity and singleness of purpose. People who have this line abhor distractions, and like to pursue a steady unaltered course. 

Two Vertical Lines show a nature of good resolves, but with divided interests-notably a balance between intellect and emotion.

Three Vertical Lines indicate a spiritual nature, as well as one which is mental and emotional; it is not a sign of achievement, but of interest in work, pleasure, and serious thought. 

Many Vertical Lines-usually small-are a sign of interest in many things; they show a versitile nature, but also indicate that the individual is not apt to centralize on single effort. They stand for scattered forces-for a person of the enthusiastic, changing nature which makes beginnings but lacks concentration. 

THE NOSE 

The nose is the most distinctive feature of man's Physiognomy; because it is an important point of difference between the human and the animal. 

Animals have eyes-which are often expressive. The human eye shows intelligence; it can hold one's gaze, and exert control, thus demonstrating its superiority over the animal eye. 

But the nose is a feature which is lacking among animals; and the ape, which parodies the human race, has no nose- 84

with the exception of the Proboscis Monkey of Borneo, a species with a very comical nasal appendage. 

Definitely developed noses have been typical of civilized races. Primitive savages are peculiarly identified by their flat, crudely-formed noses.

In Character Reading, the nose is, therefore, worthy of careful study; but the classification of different types is a matter which can become very complicated. It is better to analyze definite types of noses than to attempt a listing of the lesser variations; for the student requires knowledge that will enable him to read character quickly and effectively.

Size of the Nose 

The first impression that the Character Analyst obtains when he views a nose is that of size. Obviously all noses can be identified as Large, Small, Long, or Short. 

A Large Nose indicates a forceful individual-it is usually of the Convex Type, and has the characteristics of Profile given under that heading. A Large, prominent nose also shows an amorous disposition. The person with this type of Nose has a keen interest in the opposite sex; and the actions of such people are determined by their other characteristics-particularly those that are observed by further study of the Nose. 

A Small Nose shows a certain mildness, and a matter-of-fact view on love. People with this type of Nose are usually wellsuited to sedate married life; they avoid intrigue and do not become enthusiastic over members of the opposite sex. They never make advances, and they are accustomed to avoid compromising situations. 

A Long Nose shows caution, and an inclination to view everything conservatively. It is not a sign of a pessimistic nature; but it does indicate the quality of considering the less favorable aspects of a situation, and planning accordingly. 

Such a Nose is typical of the person who is reserved and very careful in action.

A Short Nose reveals a congenial type of person who is inclined to view the bright side of a question. People with this type of Nose can see possibilities of good results in anything they undertake. They are not exactly optimistic; but when they consider a matter they instinctively minimize anything that might interfere with their plans. They do this logically, but they are not apt to consider unfavorable possibilities. 85

CHARACTER READING The Sides of the Nose 

By the Sides of the Nose, we mean the nostrils-as they affect the shape of the Nose.

Wide Sides indicate a rather active-but sometimes childish -disposition. It is a sign of youthful emotionalism; a nature that is easily influenced by melody, harmony and rhythm. There is a certain amount of primitive nature in the Wide Sided Nose, which is particularly evident when the Nose is Large and Short. 

Wideness is a sign of good humor, and a rather happy, carefree disposition.

Narrow Sides show a disposition which is not more than normally energetic, and which is apt to be annoyed by noise and confusion. It indicates a nervousness, or dislike of too much enthusiasm, and people with this type of Nose are inclined to criticise those who delight in frivolity.

A great many bigoted persons have Narrow Noses, and this is a typical indication of the sour, disapproving nature that favors Blue Laws and becomes easily annoyed at anything that seems unconventional.

This does not mean that people with Narrow Noses all have such inclinations; those just described represent an extreme, yet at the same time, this physical characteristic is usually present in persons who hold those extreme views. 

On the contrary, Wideness does not mean that the individual will be childish and refractory. 

The actual contrast is that the Wide-Sided Nose shows a natural tendency toward exuberance and enjoyment of noise and diversion, while the Narrow-Sided Nose is disturbed and rendered nervous by too much excitement of that type. 

From such normal feelings develop the extremes of these types; and those who cannot curb their buoyancy are as deserving of criticism as those who become hypercritical. 

The End of the Nose 

Two extremes of temperament are represented by noses which are Blunt or Sharp.

The Blunt Nose is rather thick-fleshy at the end, and easily identified. It shows the trait of forcefulness; and ability to put things into action and to control a situation. 

People with this type of Nose have directive powers, but enjoy leisure quite as much as action. They do their work 86

forcefully, but are not particularly concerned over minor details.

The Sharp Nose becomes thin at the end, and is not fleshy at the point. It shows a nature which is not forceful, but which is constantly active over smaller matters, avoiding leisure and always finding something to do, even if it is necessary to deal with trivialities.

Profile Tendencies 

Viewed from the side, Noses sometimes have a noticeable Upward or Downward point. These indicate characteristics that are in accordance with Upward and Downward Feature 

Lines. 

The Upward Nose, with its "saucy" look shows a disposition which seeks knowledge of facts and is interested in human experience. It reveals rather cheerful habits, and shows interest in study. 

The Downward Nose reveals economical traits; a careful disposition in matters of money and resources, and no tendency toward cheerfulness, although persons with this type of Nose can not be classed as gloomy. 

It is a physical feature, however, that is often seen in a sour disposition.

Distinctive Types of Noses 

There are certain distinctive Types of Noses which reveal marked characteristics; and the more important of these are listed here. They are best studied by the Profile. 

High Bridged: A sign of an aristocratic, domineering nature. 

Impatience is usually a noticeable trait.

Bulging Tip: This curves forward and seems to swell at the tip. It shows a wilful nature, but good business sense. 

Slightly Raised: This type of Nose is not High-Bridged, but it is somewhat similar outward curve below the bridge of the Nose the curve often slight. It reveals a nature which does not domineer, but which loves to command; a person who seeks to obtain whatever he can get fairly, and who will willingly show independence.
