CHARACTER READING 

These lists, with more than seventy-five types of occupations, covers nearly every field. Such terms as "Craftsman,' "Laborer," "Technician," cover ordinary workmen, specialized workmen, trained men, respectively. 

Any occupation not listed can be identified with one that is in the list.

Where certain Formations, Types, etc. are omitted it is because they are neither specially adapted nor unsuited to the particular occupation. 

In Qualifications, it is understood that the particular occupation serves to bring out the useful and well-developed characteristics; while the Disqualifications are given whenever any particular 

Formation, Head, or Type of Profile is apt to display its poorer characteristics as a handicap.

SELECTIVE EMPLOYMENT 

Whatever may be the problems of the individual who is finding it difficult to choose his correct vocation, the problems of the employer are a hundred-fold.

The individual may give up the struggle, and continue to be a liability and worrk for his temporary troubles; or he may solve his difficulties and enter the selective class. But his place will be filled by someone quite as incompetent, or unsuited to the vocation chosen.

It is impossible to estimate the tremendous losses and waste sustained by large concerns and employers because of the present unscientific and inefficient ways in which employes are selected and assigned to duties.

It costs money to break in a new worker. Even a person of some experience cannot fit into a ney system of routine immediately. The industrious workers who complain because their wages are not high enough and who want a higher scale do not realize that they are helping to support incompetents. 

Every big business is, to an extent, a charitable institution in that it has people on its pay-roll who are worth virtually nothing and who must be supported until they have reached a productive stage. If everyone was intelligent, efficient and competent, the result would be greater profits for the business and higher wages for the workers.

Obviously this situation cannot be completely eliminated. 

Every concern must take in workers and support them through the 134

preliminary stages. But it is a startling fact that this is generally done on the trial and error method. It is quite all right to put a man in a job and keep him there until he has become effective; but when three, four, or five men must be tried-employed, kept, and discharged-until the right one is found, the situation is bad. 

The individual may often be excused from blame. The opportunities he has for work are comparatively few. But when a company wants new employes, it is besieged by applicants. Here are dozens—perhaps hundreds of available persons; among them are individuals who will be of high productive value. Some are chosen by a hit or miss selection, and a number of those selected prove to be incompetent. The person choosing them has committed a double mistake; he has not only taken in valueless material; he has in all probability turned away valuable human beings. 

If a concern finds waste in its mechanical departments, it corrects it. Efficiency experts are employed to make the business function at highest speed. Carefulness is used in buying materials.

Such things are systematized; yet employment goes on in the same haphazard way.

One of the gravest mistakes made by large concerns is in the discharging of employes who have been with the company a reasonable length of time. It stands to reason that a man or woman who is familiar with the methods of a concern has reached a state of value. Should such people prove incompetent in the positions which they hold, that is no sign that they are useless to the concern. Transfer from one department to another has done wonders with individuals. Competency is often a matter of interest in work.

A large concern in New York gets all its employes from the outside. If a new man or woman fails to qualify for the position for which they have been hired, they are discarded. Transfers only take place in an emergency. This particular company is so bound with red tape that the most competent person requires three months' service in order to fit into the routine. 

As a result the business loses thousands of dollars every year through disregard of the human cogs that make up its mechanism. 

People have left its employ and have immediately made good with other similar companies. This same company has experts who plan all sorts of ways of gaining results from the coordination of departments; yet they totally ignore the individual. 

The policy of that particular business any many others throughout the country is to try a lot of people until they get 135

CHARACTER READING 

the right one. They never realize that they can take a competent person and make that person right. A good employe is an asset to the concern which has his services, just as much as a delivery truck or a window display. But put the truck in the window and the display in the garage and neither one will be of use. 

The human mind understands mechanical methods. A vacuum cleaner and a sewing machine are both mechanical devices.

But one sweeps the floor and the other sews clothes. Try to use them for opposite purposes and the result will be nil. Every person of sound judgment knows that; yet men of intelligence seem to lack the ability to realize that human machines are suited to certain forms of work and not to others. 

Ask the average employer or the man who hires employment what method he uses. He will probably be stumped at first; then he will try to make a few rules. Neatness of appearance may be one-it probably will be. It will prove to be a rule that he goes by whether he is hiring a clerk or a truck-driver. Recommendations of character which can be obtained by anyone who is not a criminal; statement of religion-whether the applicant is sincere or a hypocrite; previous experience-whether the prospective employe has been a worker or a loafer; these are the clumsy, inefficient methods used in choosing employes.

References are superficial; religion is apart from work; experience is misleading. Some persons learn more about a business in three months than others do in three years. 

Why bother with what an individual writes or says about himself; whether he comes to get a job before he has time to dress, or whether he happens to have put on his Sunday suit just to make an impression? Employers use these methods of judgment because they know no other. They overlook the fact that the individual brings the required references with him, and that they can be read clearly through Character Analysis.

An employer is looking for people who will either fit into a certain definite job or who will be of future value to his business. 

He can choose the proper persons from any number of applicants by studying their Character. Through scientific Character Read ing it is possible to classify the individual exactly. 

This cold fact has been realized by more than one industry. 

Large concerns are now using Character Analysis as the only sure and efficient method of scientific employment. Those concerns that are not adopting the same method are as far behind the others as they would be in a mechanical way if they were using hand- 136

work instead of machinery; horses and wagons instead of motortrucks; or shipping by canal boat instead of by railroad.

The Vocational Charts preceding this section are useful to the employer as well as to the individual who wants to find the right job. Through them, one can learn the best qualifications of an individual for a certain type of work. 

By applying them to present employes, the employer can learn which ones are suited to the jobs they are doing. He will see the opportunity for effective changes; he will increase the working power of his greatest asset.

Where two persons seem equally fitted for a job; or where further guidance is required, Physiognomy and the other means of Character Reading will prove of additional value.

Every employer should analyze his workers; he should use 

Character Reading as the scientific way of measuring the value of the human mechanism.

Inasmuch as every job requires certain general qualities in the individual, special charts are given herewith, showing the listing of Formation, Heads and Physiognomy in such qualifications. 

The chart appears on the next page.

SELLING BY CHARACTER READING 

Besides the choice of a vocation, and the matter of employment, there is a third important use of Character Reading. That is the relationship between two individuals, in business matters. 

The one phase of contact that is of constant importance today is selling. Men and women sell products; and they sell their own personalities.

Selling is not merely the job of placing an order of merchandise; it is the delivering of an idea; the placing of a new projectanything in fact, where one person must convince another that something is feasible and useful.

People get jobs by selling their ideas and their personalities; and everyone who is commercially successful must employ salesmanship at some time in some tangible way. 

In fact, salesmanship is often most important to the man who employs it least often. The professional salesman usually depends upon frequency or quantity of sales. The man who does not sell for a living may have an idea which will make him a fortune if he puts it over. He must sell it—and it may be the only important sale of his life.

The expression "I'm sold" is applied more by the man who accepts an idea than by the man who orders a quantity of merchandise. Selling is vital to the progressive person.

We have listed the qualities that show native ability in selling, and those which indicate a lack of such talent; and these are applicable to vocational guidance or choice of men for selesmen. 

But the art of selling, itself, whether employed by the professional salesman or the man who sells seldom, depends upon a knowledge or study of the buyer.

It is quite safe to say that the man with few qualifications as a salesman can, by analyzing the Character of his clients, gain greater results than the natural salesman who merely relies on instinctive ability. Even the man who has traits adverse of salesmanship can get results on the rare occasions when he must sell by using Character Reading as his guide. 

As for the professional salesman-he can more than double his production if he employs scientific ways of working. The average salesman of pleasing personality often gets a stereotyped way of "breaking the ice" which works with many people but not with all. He will tell of "tough customers" whom he cannot sell; 139

CHARACTER READING 

and the reason for his lack of success is because they are not responsive to his standardized method of selling. 

The best salesmen are those who employ different tactics with different individuals; who make a real study of the person they approach. In brief, the man who can use Character Reading in his selling is the one who will get the greatest results. 

Every sales manager should study Character Analysis and pass the knowledge on to his subordinates. Individual salesmen should take up the subject; and they will immediately reap remarkable benefits.

The man who has been ineffective in selling, or who must employ salesmanship rather frequently will find Character Analysis of inestimable value. It is obvious that he lacks a certain knack, and is inexperienced in selling. The approach is his greatest difficulty. With a sure guide to the correct method, what more can he desire?

The man who may have to put ideas across- -who wants to make the best impression with his superiors or with his associates, needs Character Analysis as a fundamental method. Upon everyone evolves the duty of creating a good impression, whether it is money, reputation, or social position that is involved. 

All people have likes and dislikes-prejudices and foibles. 

The man or woman who can recognize those traits in the individual is on the road to success. This is selling personality—and it can be done through a knowledge of scientific Character Reading.

We can call this art "Selling and Persuading," thus covering the full field-whether money is involved or not. 

Selling the Masculine Type: Men, or those of the Masculine Type are impressed by rather direct methods. They want to know what a proposition is, and they are not interested by unimportant details. They like something that has a purpose and they listen to the person who sits down and unfolds a project in a business-like way.

Selling the Feminine Type: Those of the Feminine Type like to listen to something that is presented in a social, friendly way. They are impressed by the recommendations of mutual acquaintances; by the fact that so-and-so has done this or that. 

They are unimpressed by a business-like atmosphere. It disturbs them, and they like little details and trivialities that have no bearing on the project itself. 140

Selling the Blonde Type: Here one must appeal to enthusiasm and optimism. It is important that the seller or persuader should show these qualities. Blondes are active amid the affairs of the world, and have a diversity of interest. 

They are impressed by the man or woman who can show travel and experience in the past, and they like to listen to those who are emphatic and have done big things. They like display, and new, experimental ideas.

The salesman who says that his business is good makes an immediate impression on the Blonde. Business is the topic that interests them-if one is talking business. They keep business and home affairs different. They are interested in the seller's business experiences because that is how they judge him. If he has sold Mr. X the capitalist, that is more important than if the seller happens to be a close friend of Mr. Z the financier. 

The Blonde buyer will give preference to the man who is on the job and who can show the best proposition. He is not sentimental. He can be loaded up with ideas or merchandise for he will find ways of disposing of them. 

Stick to business with the Blonde Type and keep away from personalities. Be breezy and alert; allow him to make his own decision, and get him on the dotted line while he is in the mood. 

Do not ignore him or make him feel that other business is more important. He wants to deal with the person who is wholehearted and giving the most efficient service.

Selling the Brunette Type: Here is the person who is inter ested in personalities, who is swayed by sentimental reasons, and who, although slow to grasp an idea, will retain it longer and more effectively than the Blonde.

Friendship and mutual acquaintances are important with the Brunette. He is impressed by the person who is interested in his family and his social activities. He likes to hear an expression of personal interest.

He likes to hear slow, well-put remarks concerning things which are reliable and proven. If he is buying Life Insurance, he will be interested in standard policies-not in new trick policies which are similar but "improved"—although such ideas appeal to the Blonde.

It is not business, but sentiment that is in back of his inner feelings. The fact that the seller is a friend of X is more important than that the seller has made a big sale to Z. 141

CHARACTER READING 

The Brunette is a repeat buyer. If he is sold by one person he will give that seller preference in the future. Time is needed to convince him, and he is bewildered or unimpressed by many details. He will stick to a decision once he has made it. The seller can tactfully and artfully make decisions for the Brunette. 

A friendly, well-put suggestion will settle the doubtfulness in his mind, and he will agree. 

Selling the Mental Type: Here one must appeal to reason, to idealism, and mentality. The man or woman of the Mental 

Type is interested in beauty and romance.

These people are impressed by anything which is idealistic and educational. They have scruples against anything that seems doubtful or crafty.

They are impressed by the education and sincerity of the seller. They like to listen to a man who is reasoning, and who creates the impression of honesty and who holds up his product or his idea because it is high and of quality. 

There are, of course, some of the Mental Type who have become dishonest and crafty; but they are comparatively few and can be easily recognized. 

Selling the Motive Type: These persons are interested in big ideas that show action and power. They want products that are durable and they listen to the man who represents a firm or an idea that is up and doing.

They are independent and challenging. It is rash to make statements to them that cannot be proven. They will listen to facts, and are impressed by them; but they will hold absurd estimates against the seller.

They like the salesman who is firm, definite, and physically active. They are domineering and like combat; hence they appreciate the person who meets them on their own ground in a friendly way. But the seller who starts out timidly is lost. 

Selling the Vital Type: Here are people with commercial ideas, who want to see just how a project or a proposition will be of value to them.

They must be convinced that they can use what they can get. 

The Mental will be sold on the ideal proposition, whether or not it is entirely practical; the Motive will listen to an appeal that shows effort on his part, and will be ready to show individual action; but the Vital wants something that fits in with his present plans. 142

He is friendly, easy to approach, and a good listener; but all the time he is wondering just how he can use the proposition or merchandise that is being offered to him. 

The best way to reach the Vital Type of person is to find out what he wants and give it to him. If he has had success through the exploitation of an idea or a commodity, he will be interested in something like it. Briefly, he wants more of a good thing. 

Selling the Mental Motive: Price is not a great consideration with the Mental-Motive. He wants the best, regardless of price. 

He is a man of action who can dispose of goods or exploit an idea provided he has faith in the product. 

He has a wide variety of interests; is impressed by the person who can show the same. He will talk of golf and sports along with business, and likes the salesman who is intelligent and active. 

This is the type of person to meet on his own ground; to become actively friendly with him. Money is never his primary consideration. If a product seems good, durable, and of value, he will listen to reasoning ways of handling it. He likes to hear what has been done by others, for he has confidence in his own ability to do likewise. 

Selling the Mental Vital: This is the man of commercial ideals. He wants things that are good, yet practical. He is not satisfied with a proposition purely because it will make money; he wants something that will add to the reputation of his business. 

Primarily he listens to a good, sound, intelligent idea; and selling him is therefore a double job. For after he has been convinced that the proposition or the merchandise is right, he will begin to figure it out in terms of profit. 

More disappointments are experienced in selling persons of this type than any other; for after their minds seem very favorable they will discover something that will make them give a negative decision.

It takes a double-barreled gun to handle the Mental-Vital Type. Intelligent talk, good appearance and sincerity are essential in the approach, and they must be followed with sound business ideas-facts and figures.

The Mental-Vital will listen to reason, however, and he will often allow his interest in a good proposition carry it through. He likes to make suggestions; to present possible variations; to meet the seller half way; and it is always advisable to have a counterproposition on hand. 143

CHARACTER READING 

Selling the Motive Vital: This fellow deals in close figures; cents rather than dollars. He doesn't care how ideal a proposition may be; whether the goods are fine or junk. He wants something that will be of use to him; something that will show a quick definite return. 

He is interested in active ways of merchandising; buying articles in great quantity and turning them over with a small but certain profit. He will buy old goods cheap and find some way of unloading. He is friendly but active, and is impressed by the salesman who is the same way.

Selling the High Head: The High Headed man is dignified and proud; he must be approached by someone who can take account of this. No back-slapping will do. Business relations should be formal and polite.

Selling the Low Head: Roughness, and primitive methods make no difference with this fellow. He likes to be complimented and flattered. He is crude in his ideas, and although he may argue, the persistent active seller will win out. 

Selling the Long Head: The Long Headed man is percep tive and emotional. He will find flaws very quickly, and the seller must have all his knowledge at his finger-tips. Behind a rather cold disposition, this man is friendly. His emotions and his sentiments can be reached. 

But if the head has Long Front only, sentiment is not well developed, and it will take a great display of facts and often frequent visits to overcome the sales resistance of this type. 

When the Head has Long Rear development, appeal to friendship and sentiment is quickly granted. The individual will not be at all challenging and will like the man who approaches him in a friendly, easy-going manner. 

Selling the Short Head: The Short Headed man keeps sen timent out of business, and will be badly impressed by the seller who seeks this form of appeal. He is grasping and unsympathetic and wants to get what he can out of any proposition. 

Show him where he is making a saving, or getting the best of a deal, and he will be interested. 

Selling the Wide Head: This type of man is aggressive and blunt. He will throw an immediate challenge at the seller. He tries to get rid of anyone who is trying to convince him of anything, and does not want to grant a hearing.

The only way to handle him is to gain an audience by some method and to launch into a quick, rapid, aggressive statement 144

of business. The approach must be quick and firm, with decision. Once he bgins to listen, he will calm down and talk sensibly.

Selling the Narrow Head: This type of person often has an inferiority complex, and is ready to buy anything that is offered in a superior, convincing way. He will give excuses, express doubts, and seek to put off a decision, for his purpose is to escape the seller and forget him. Persisitence is effective, and the seller should get what he wants on the first interview. 

Remember that this type may promise anything in order to get away. He will dodge the seller, and try every artifice to avoid repeat visits if the business is not accomplished on the first occasion. 

Selling and Profile

The Convex Forehead shows the person who listens for any false statement by the seller. He remembers everything that is said and offers objections. The faults he finds must be met by counter arguments.

The Concave Forehead shows the good thinker who will be very slow to decide, and who will be hard to convince. 

The Plane Forehead shows the man who is impressed by sound reasoning but who gradually becomes less and less convinced if his doubts begin to work.

The Convex Eye shows the person who must be out-talked. 

Quick, fast conversation is his method. The seller must say much and talk in great variety until he finally begins to land. 

The Concave Eye shows the person who says very little, and who will not easily show approval or disapproval. Such people are often thoroughly convinced when they do not appear to be.

The Plane Eye indicates the man who is well-informed or who wants to be, and he is anxious to understand a subject thoroughly. No sensible amount of explanation is wasted on him. 

The Convex Nose shows the person who will say "Yes" and mean it, or "No" and mean it. He must be handled rather quickly and in a business-like way, for he will end everything when he makes his decision. He

The Concave Nose is the man who is slow to decide. will keep putting it off until some later time. He is never lost by the seller, but he is hard to win. 145

CHARACTER READING 

The Plane Nose shows the person who will not want to leave his work to talk about buying. He must be impressed by the importance of a matter-that it will not take long. He will want to postpone a decision, but if he is convinced that immediate decision is important, he will make it.

The Convex Mouth indicates the impatient person who will grumble and act unpleasantly, yet who can be forced to listen to reason if the seller is pointed and definite in his remarks. He will always have something else to do; the seller must make him forget it.

The Concave Mouth shows the person who is pleasant, patient, and a ready listener, but whose appearance is deceiving when results are concerned. The seller should deal with him carefully, and purposefully, using the person's patience to advantage.

The Plane Mouth is the type of person who is not over-difficult to convince, but who will grow suddenly impatient if the seller has too long a story. 

The Convex Chin shows the person who is shrewd and who must be aroused to immediate interest. He can be hurried into a favorable turn of mind.

The Concave Chin indicates the person who is very quiet and who does not betray his feelings. If he finally says he is unconvinced, he must be handled in different way, for once he has formed an opinion he keeps it. He does not like to be questioned.

The Plane Chin shows the person who calculates on a proposition and can be sold at the right moment. It is important to the seller to know when to stop. It is very easy to talk one's self in and then out with such a person. 

Physiognomy in Selling 

A knowledge of Physiognomy is the greatest of assets in selling, particularly to the professional salesman.

The characteristics shown by Formation, Head, and Profile are, of course, essential; but they merely give the efficient seller a general basis to work upon. 

A great percentage of the most important sales that are made are those which follow a first interview, or which take place during a long discussion.

In a general way, the seller can classify his subject quickly enough to make the approach and to adopt a method of discussion. But when he has encountered an important prospect or a 146

difficult type, he must employ every possible way in which to convince the person.

Here is the use of Physiognomy. The story told by an eyebrow; by lines in the forehead; by the shape of the eye, or the formation of the lips-these are the important items that reveal the traits of the person, and enable the seller to judge him as an individual rather than as a member of a type.

No particular classification of these distinctions can serve as a definite guide; the important matter is to study Physiognomy, and to familiarize oneself with it; and then to use it actively as the inspiration in selling.

Through Physiognomy, the salesman can learn when he has said enough; whether his arguments are convincing; or whether he should change his tactics. For the thoughts of the individual are recorded in his features.

Other Means of Analysis: Through Posture and Gesture, and other means of Character Analysis, important traits are revealed to the observant person. The fundamentals of Phrenology and Chirognomy should be given careful attention. 

The clever salesman will always note the hands of the person to whom he is talking. For there he will find indications that are of special value.

CONCLUSION Having learned the recognized methods of Character Read ing, and being familiar with the uses of the science, it behooves the reader or the student to put his knowledge to practical use. 

This can be done partially; by degrees, or completely. But to reach its fullest extent of usefulness, Character Analysis should be treated thoroughly and scientifically.

This is best accomplished by the employment of a record. 

Persons can be studied at different times; it is not essential to try to make a complete survey of all features at once. 

Very often some trait of Posture or Gesture will make itself evident at an unexpected time.

While Character Reading at a glance is of essential value, the student who proceeds further and keeps records of persons whom he wishes to understand will learn a great deal more than if he concludes his survey with the Preliminary Analysis.

The record blanks which follow are planned for the purpose of thorough analysis, and the more completely they are kept, the better will they serve their purpose. 147

How To Become Prosperous FIRMS DRON VS NAILS/ By ARTHUR GOULD 

Prosperity is the great goal sought by all kinds and conditions of men. And why should it not be so? It includes much that makes for comfort, happiness, content and well-being-and who among us is not seeking these things and striving to attain them? 

To be prosperous is to be a success instead of a failure; to be comfortable, instead of

HOW TO BECOME 

PROSPEROUS uncomfortable; to be independent, instead 

BY ARTHUR GOULD 

of dependent upon others. In short, Prosperity is to be worth while to ourselves and to others dependent upon us, instead of having been found lacking when weighed in the scales of achievement. Prosperity brings with it the wherewithal to possess clothing, food, shelter, books, music, travel, necessities, comforts and luxuries. Lack of 

Prosperity means slavery to circumstances and environment, or to other men. Prosperity means freedom, independence, liberty, ability to express one's self freely and to manifest in action and outward from those ideas and ideals of which we have long dreamt.

This book teaches the Natural Laws of Prosperity. It holds that 

Prosperity is the natural right of mankind, and that only an ignorance of Nature's laws prevents any person of average intelligence from attaining it. It shows that just as Nature provides for the nourishment of the plant and the animal, so it provides for the financial nourishment of mankind. It shows the operation of a great natural law operating in this direction, and which, when taken advantage of, enables one to receive his rightful share of the good things of life. It teaches of the existence of the great 

Law of Attraction, by means of which the individual is correlated to the objects which he needs for his development and well-being. 

It shows how successful men attract to themselves by this law the things, people, and circumstances necessary for their success. It shows that this Law of Attraction is as universal as is the Law of Gravitation, and it points out the way by means of which this law may be applied in one's everyday life.

This book shows that the Flame of Desire generates the Steam of Will, and thereby sets in operation the powers of the individual. It shows how to feed this flame, and to keep it burning brightly and fiercely. It shows how many men lack the ability to desire with sufficient strength, and it teaches them how to remedy this defect. It teaches the value of Ideation. It shows the creative energy of the Ideal. It instructs one in the creation and employment of Mental Images which tend to materialize in objective form and power. It shows how one should first create the Ideal Image of that which he wishes, in order to afterward materialize it in reality. It teaches the dynamic force of Faith and Hope, and the part played by the same in the work of the Law of Attraction. It shows how Faith and Hope often are really Fate and Destiny in disguise. The psychol ogy of Faith and Hore in their relation to Prosperity is carefully and clearly explained. The Dynamic Power of Confident Expectation is made clear.

Astrology is the most ancient of all the sciences. Unfortunately, up to the present, the abstruse nature of the books about it has kept the public in ignorance of its real claims, and has permitted all sorts of fraud and folly to flourish under its name.

SCIENCE OF BREATH By Yogi Ramacharaka centuries from teacher to pupil, and gradually a definite Yogi science was built up. To these investigations and teachings was finally applied the term "Yoga." 

Yoga is divided into several branches, ranging from that which teaches the control of body, to that which teaches the attainment of the highest spiritual development.

The Hindu Yogis have always paid great attention to the Science of Breath, for reasons which will be apparent to the student who reads this book. Many 

Western writers have touched upon this phase of the Yogi teaching, but we believe that it has been reserved for the writer of this work to give to the Western student, in concise form and simple language, the underlying principles of the Yogi Science of Breath, together with many of the favorite 

Yogi breathing exercises and methods.

The first part of the book is devoted to the physical phase of the Science of Breath; then the psychic and mental sides are considered, and finally the spiritual side is touched upon.

A great deal of Yogi lore is condensed in a few pages.

Philosophy

Digest of the Works of ANDREW JACKSON DAVIS Greatest Seer of Modern 

Times No one can deny that Davis was a wonderful seer, the greatest American seer. He was one of the first to explain the mighty understanding forces in and around us.

The popularity of the writings of Andrew Jackson Davis, among spiritualists and numerous schools of religious mystics, has been one of the phenomenal facts of the last half century. Investigators, however, have frequently found great difficulty in studying these physical revelations, owing to the fact that they are comprised in twenty-seven large volumes, and that no concise summary of the teaching comprised in them has hitherto been given to the world within any moderate compass. The object of this work has been to present an impartial summary of the whole teaching of Andrew Jackson Davis, as far as possible, without diminution of any kind. It is hoped this attempt to summarize the teachings of this famous seer, and thus render them accessible to a far wider public than hitherto will meet a need of the present generation which is eagerly seeking for more facts which may throw some clearer light upon the mysteries of the other world. 

His doctrine, in a word, was that the world beyond is as natural as this world of ours; that it is neither the Heaven nor hell of official 

Christianity; that men and women in their psychic bodies have opportunities for progress and for better environment. In a word the gospel of Davis cast out all fear concerning the life to come.

THE SOLAR PLEXUS OR Abdominal Brain By THERON Q. DUMONT THE SOLAR PLEXUS Theron Q. Dumont 

Man has four brains, and not merely one, as is commonly believed to be the case. Each of the four brains have separate characteristics and distinctive offices and functions.

THE SOLAR PLEXUS, or ABDOMINAL BRAIN 

is situated in the upper part of the abdomen, behind the stomach, in front of the great artery, and in front of pillars of the diaphragm.

THE SOLAR PLEXUS is the great plexus, i. e., network of nerve-fibres, mass of nerve-substance, etc., of the great Sympathetic Nervous System. It is composed of both gray and white nervous substance, or brain-matter, similar to that of the other three brains of man. It receives and distributes nerve-impulses and currents to all the abdominal organs, and supplies the main organs of nutrition, assimilation, etc., with nervous energy.

IT PERFORMS A MOST IMPORTANT WORK 

supplying the nerve-energy which is required for the process of nutrition, assimilation, growth, etc. In fact, it is the great powerhouse of physical life-energy. 

The bodily functions cannot be performed without it; when it is injured the entire physical well-being is at once seriously affected; when it receives a severe shock, death often ensues. 

Its name, "solar," was bestowed upon it by reason of its central position; the fact that its filaments extend in all directions to the important abdominal organs, like the rays of the sun; and the fact that it is recognized as being the power-house, and great reservoir of "life force," just as the sun is the great power-house and reservoir of material energy of our solar system. 

Not alone modern scientific investigators; but also many very ancient investigators, such as the oriental occultists and sages, who many centuries ago recognized certain subtle functions and offices of this wonderful "fourth brain" of man, and taught their students many valuable methods of effectively employing its finer forces and hidden energies. in this book will be found the essence and fundamental substance of these higher teachings concerning the Solar Plexus or Abdominal Brain-the Fourth 

Brain of Man! How to arouse the attention of the Solar Plexus. How to "wake up" the Solar Plexus. How to proceed when you have Awakened the Solar

Plexus. How to cause the Solar Plexus to restore health. How to gain Vital Forces and Physical Energy by means of the Solar Plexus. 

Wonderful Instructions, Full Exercises, Practical Methods and Everything You Should Know About the Solar Plexus.

The Astrologer's Planetary Hour Indicator 

THE AST THE ASTROLO 

DIA HIA VI VII ROLOGER'S HOUR NDICATOR PLANETARY F. SEWARD TOR SHOWS THE GNS THAT HAR 30 THE HOURS THE PLANETS 

VARD & CO. 

WILTON AVE CAGO, ILLI 

"A group of indicators photographed one-half actual size, embossed in gold, and are indeed very attractive."

The value of Planetary Hours has been known for ages, but before the invention of the Indicator, the Astrologer was obliged to compute the fortunate and unfortunate hours for each day from a set of especially prepared charts or tables, which, of course, while very accurate and valuable, yet the great amount of detail work necestary in the preparation of such tables naturally made the price prohibitive to many who otherwise would have used them. According t oAstrological theories and aus picious time for the commencement of any affair is an important matter, and As rologers generally claim anything once commenced, its existence will be of a nature Corresponding to the condition under which it was begun; by beginning things on a good day and in a fortunate hour their existence is more likely to prove satisfactory and profitable, whereas if begun on a bad day and in an hour ruled by a malefo planet they are most sure to prove very unsatisfactory and troublesome. 

The late Prof. L. D. Broughton, in his book, entitled "The Elements of Astrology," says: "Probably there is nothing more convincing of the general belief in 

Astrology throughout the whole civilized world in former ages than the names of the days of the week, and the planetary hours governing them, and which we continue 

(Continued on next Pages

to make use of even to the present day. • Indeed, in every civilized or half. civilized nation on the face of the earth they have the names of the days of the week called after the planets and have them arranged according to the planetary hours. It is an utter impossibility for those days to have been arranged in 'he order which they are by chance, or without a knowledge of the planetary hours: therefore, those learned men who first arranged the days in the week must have had a practical knowledge in Astrology, and such as but few learned men at the present day possess, and there must have been thousands of Astrologers in every nation and in all parts of the world which claimed to be at all civilized, to have had such an influence with mankind as to have made the names of those to become universal, or to be adopted by all civilized nations. There is nothing to be compared to it in religion, science, politics, or even fashions; and, as Prof. Proctor says, 'Astrology has a most respectable origin, and if common opinion could prove any doctrine just, Astrology must, it would seem, have been based on truth.'" 

The influence of the Sun and Moon are accepted facts. The Sun is recognized as influencing light, heat and life, the Moon's influence (cycle of 28 days) is known to affect the tides, crops, the propagation of human and animal life and the mental and physical condition in man's organism. These stupendous facts are rapidly and constantly turning the attention of investigating minds to a more careful analysis. 

The Seven Days of the Week are Named from the Planets 

In the review of ancient history we learn that the Chaldeans were the originators of mathematics, astrology and astronomy and were the first people to compute the calendar, dividing the year into months, months into weeks, weeks into days, also divided the days into hours, hours into minutes and minutes into seconds. 

Surely it is an indisputable fact that the names of the days of the week are universal and were taken from the names of Heavenly bodies. In the English tongue they were based on Norse and Saxon roots-Sunday (Sun's Day), Monday (Moon's 

Day), Tuesday (Tiw's Day), Wednesday (Woden's Day), Thursday (Thor's Day), and

Friday (Frigga's Day), although Saturday seems to be as well derived from the Latin 

Saturnus as from the Saxon Saterne. 

In Latin the names are: Dies Solis (Sol's Day), Dies Lunae (Luna's Day), Dies 

Martis (Mar's Day), Dies Mercurii (Mercury's Day), Dies Jovis (Jove's Day), Dies 

Veneris (Venus' Day), Dies Saturni (Saturn's Day). In French the Latin roots are discernible in the names Dimanche, Lundi, Mardi, Mercredi, Jeudi, Vendredi, Samedis.

A very curious astrological arrangement connected each of the twenty-four hours of the day, as well as each day of the week, with one of the planets, the order of succession always remaining the same and running in this wise: Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, Sun, Venus, Mercury, Moon.

It is said that Bishop Butler, who, after writing and doing all he could to denounce Astrology, undertook to study the science in order that he might become more proficient in condemning and denouncing it. According to his own claims he became so fascinated and interested in the work that he finally wound up by writing and publishing one of the best books ever written on Astrology. 

Astronomical theories have from time to time been subject to change, but the Astrological interpretation of the order and influence of the planets has never varied. 

The facts in this matter have repeated and again repeated themselves century after century, until the knowledge which they represent has become established as science to those who have studied it. It is noticeable that those who have studied Astrology are always ready to recommend it to others, while those who have not as yet mastered its teachings are the only ones who speak disparagingly about it! 

Any person who will study Astrology will soon become so convinced with its wonderful truths that they will never again condemn it. 

In every nation of the earth today, even among those who were separated by great distances and periods of time, the Planetary Hours have maintained the same order of rotation and each people, down the pages of history to the present age, have attributed identically the same influence to each of the planets. 

Therefore it is a matter of great importance that we select a fortunate hour for the beginning of all important undertakings such as seeking employment, entering a new position or business, getting married, travel by land or sea, writing letters, as well as good hours, for study, concentration, etc. The Indicator will show the time when things started will turn out advantageously, whereas if one commenced un important affair and unconsciously chose an evil hour for the beginning, the results would be correspondingly unfortunate, according to Astrology. 

(Continued on next Page)

Interpretation of the Influence Received from the Seven Planets Which Rule the Days and Hours in the Order Named The Sun Gives a Positive Hour and Rules Sunday 

The hour of the Sun represents all affairs in which dignity, power and influence are concerned, therefore this hour should be used for all dealings with great and influential men, such as senators, governors, mayors, judges, government officials, corporations, civic authorities trustees, and all who have superior power and who hold prominent and authoritative positions. Sun hours are good for asking favors, writing letters, signing papers, such as Jeeds, wills, leases, contracts, for that which is done in a Sun hour is very apt to stand. This hour is also good for seeking promotion, recovering lost articles, and is a most ecellent hour for spiritual unfoldment as well as making new acquaintances, people are light hearted, care-free during Sun hours. 

The hour generally gives ambition and gain through enterprise and responsibility. 

The conversation at this hour is usually pertaining to business matters, employment, promotion, political affairs, etc.

The Moon Gives a Negative Hour and Rules Monday 

The hour of the Moon is good for many purposes, traveling on water, beginning short journeys, dealing with servants and all domestic affairs, making research, new acquaintances, visiting, etc. A friend made during a Moon hour is usually a friend indeed. People who deal with the general public are usually fortunate during this hour, and while Moon hours are changeable yet they are favorable to all movable things and people, such as travel, travelers, visiting, agents, shipping and for all business pertaining to liquids, such as oils, etc., and for all matters not intended as binding or permanent. It is a good hour for removals and changes which are to be temporary and not those which are intended as fixed or final. People as a rule are wavering during Moon hours and inclined to change things about and in fact can easily be induced to change their minds. Matters considered favorable should be closed up at once and not left unfinished as the opportunity may be forever lost. 

Agreements made in a Moon hour should be put in writing, as promises are not reliable, and people generally will be found uncertain and changeable at this time. 

They are always restless, discontented, indefinite, rambling and uncertain. The conversation at this hour usually pertains to travel, removals, and all matters of a common and changeable nature.

The Planet Mars Gives a Positive Hour and Rules Tuesday 

The hours of Mars is in many ways considered an evil one, although not as bad as Saturn. The influence of this planet during the time of its ruling tends to make one unusually ambitious, courageous, brave, daring and resolute. It also tends to increase the activity, force and vitality. Mars hours are parexcellent for all tasks requiring great strength and muscular activity. In fact, it is a fruitful hour for all fiery, bold, courageous transactions in which dangerous results are likely to follow -an hour when one should avoid quarrels and controversies, as well as keep away from dangerous places and people, for during Mars hours it is an easy matter to get into serious trouble; for it is an hour when one feels war-like and independent, at such times people are not apt to submit to dictation. Many calamities, fires, wrecks and accidents occur during Mars hours.

Mars rules over steel, iron and acids and persons employed with such products are usually very active during Mars hours. All conversations are usually spirited, bold, argumentative and often lead to quarrels and misunderstandings. 

The Planet Mercury Gives a Negative Hour and Rules Wednesday 

The hour of Mercury is especially favorable for occult and scientific meditation; auring the rule of Mercury hours people are inclined to be talkative, easily approached and changeable, in fact, can easily be influenced and made to change their opinions. It stimulates mental activity, gives ready response to new ideas, gives clear perception, making one quick, active and business-like, and one becomes more fluent in speech as well as prolific with the pen, and therefore is a good hour to write letters, contracts and advertisements, in fact a gooc hour for all things re quiring a quick mind, tact, or nimbleness and dexterity in execution. This hour, while in many ways uncertain, yet it is good for all things which are required to be done quickly. It is also a good hour for removals and changes which are to be temporary, and not those which are intended fixed or final. 

---

The Planet Jupiter Gives a Positive Hour and Rules Thursday 

The hour of Jupiter is in many ways similar to that of the Sun and considering it as a whole is one of the most favorable hours, being unusually good for the commencement of most all new undertakings; in fact, all important affairs should be launched in Jupiter hours if possible. It is particularly a good hour to deal with judges, lawyers, bankers, brokers and business men in general, also all matters relating to philanthropic, charitable, religious affairs. 

The hour of Jupiter conduces to clear, sound, dispassionate, charity, able and correct judgment, inclining to benevolence, honesty and justice as well as giving a tendency to become more jovial and hopeful, this hour is particularly good for asking favors, seeking employment, collecting bills, buying, speculating, gambling, races and games of chance. In fact, people are usually found to be lenient, generous and open-hearted during the hours ruled by Jupiter. Conversation during this hour usually bears upon money matters, profits and gain. 

The Planet Venus Gives a Positive Hour and Rules Friday 

The hour of Venus is especially good for dealings with the opposite sex, proposing marriage, asking favors, etc., as the tendency is to readily respond to sympathy and affection as well as show appreciation for all things beautiful. In Venus hours the sexes respond to each other more freely, and in fact is an ideal hour for pushing love affairs, courtship and marriage, special favors and privileges are more readily granted during the Venus hours than at any other time; it is a time to make new acquaintances and approach those of the opposite sex who have been heretofore unresponsive, cold and distant, in fact should strive to make the most of the beneficent influence of this hour by improving one's business and social standing; the conversa. tion during the Venus hours usually relates to art, music, dress and love affairs and brings into prominence the things which appeal to the domestic or feminine side of life.

The Planet Saturn Gives a Positive Hour and Rules Saturday 

The hour of Saturn is in many ways considered an evil one, as it is barren and deceitful and seldom anything good occurs at this time. The conversation during 

Saturn hours generally relates to things of a weary, earthly, melancholy, gloomy nature such as sickness and deaths. The hour generally brings about transactions with mistrustful, conservative, elderly people.

A proposition made for the first in a Saturn hour should not be accepted, for, as a rule, it will only result in many disappointments. Do not trust people in this hour, nor rely upon professed friendship, for if you do you will surely regret it. 

Practice concentration, auto-suggestion and mental healing. Study organization and economics or any other matter of a profound, philosophical or scientific character. 

It is not a good hour in which to begin anything new, especially if quick results or speedy profit is desired, for anything commenced at this time usually brings regret, and eventually creates a great deal of impatience through delays, hindrance, limita tions, reversals, lack of suitable opportunities, disappointments, loss, and a train of unfavorable circumstances follow in its wake, in fact fate seems to disfavor anything set in motion during the hours of Saturn, discouragement, melancholy or gloomy forebodings as it is during the Saturn hours that self-destruction and suicide most frequently occur. 

In God's plan of the universe adverse times are just as necessary as good times for the purpose of growth and it is useful to know these times so that proper discrimination and judgment may be applied. Not that we may improve nature, but that we may improve our manifestation of it. Hours which are commonly called adverse or malefic are not of themselves necessarily bad, but they indicate times in which one is apt to use poor judgment and be careful and cautious of what they do, what they think, where they go and with whom they come in contact. Hours which are termed beneficent denote times in which one should go forth confidently and give out energy directly toward the accomplishment of desires, needs and necessities. By observing these times and acting accordingly one is consciously working with nature and therefore more likely to meet with good results. 

According to Astrology, the Planetary Hour Indicator will serve as a means for practical observation, for by it one can see the time and the reason for the appearance of different attitudes, moods and states of mind throughout each and every day. By watching the hours closely one will notice that in a Saturn hour the mind takes a cautious turn and dwells upon matters of acquisition and safety with regard to possessions and the necessities of life and it becomes more serious, grave and profound in its contemplations and at times even going as far as gloom, depression and melancholy; it is the hour of weariness and general dullness, while in the very next hour-that of Jupiter-the mind no longer seeks to contract but to expand; it becomes broader in its views and covers a larger scope for extended action toward so 

---

eial and financial development and growth; the hour of Mars conduces to muscular activity to active endeavor toward strengthening the position in every manner, and through force if necessary, as well as strengthening the will and determination; the 

Sun bestows nobility, pride and a sympathetic generosity, the quality of which is gauged by the native's state of development; Venus gives love of beauty and pleasure and during the reign of her hours the mind is more ready to respond to all that is bright and gay than at any other time; Mercury leads the mind to mental activity, new ideas come easiest in a Mercury hour; the Moon turns the mind to public and domestic affairs and matters connected with the common things of life, and to changes generally. 

Note the time and refer to the Indicator and see what hour is ruling when you receive either good or bad news, or when you accomplish something particularly satisfactory, and in this way you will soon learn the value of planetary hours. 

Test these influences by starting some new undertaking in a Saturn hour and note how it will drag along or fail; then again start some deal in a Mars hour and see if you do not have trouble with it before you get through. Note how restless and uneasy people are in a Moon hour. Call on your employer or some influential person in a Sun hour and see if he does not grant your request promptly if within the bounds of reason. If you have an outstanding account call on the party in a Jupiter hour and see if he does not pay you a part of the account or at least tell you when he will pay. Dun him in a Mars hour and note how touchy and independent he becomes. And again if you want to speculate or play games of chance select the proper hours if you wish to win. Note the movements of stocks on the Board of Trade and see them go up and down during the different hours. The same mode of reasoning also applies to buying, selling and changing employment-one time you can secure a number of positions and again you could not get a job, no matter how hard you try. 

If you write a letter in a Moon hour and should you for any reason keep it for a few hours or over night, you would in all probability change your mind and not send it at all; and then again you make up your mind to make a purchase or perhaps give up your position and then change your mind completely and say, "What a fool I would have been." Surely there must be a natural law for these modes of rest and unrest. Is it not plain to you that everything in God's universe runs in Cycles?

We have every reason to believe that this Indicator will at once become a marvel with speculators, merchants, salesmen and business people in general, for if you are anxious to know whether it is best to take up a business transaction; make a journey or know the results of any important matter you have only to consult the Indicator, and you will know at a glance whether or not it is a favorable time according to astrology for your action.

We believe that if you will test the Indicator for a few days, you will be highly pleased with the practical good and amusement your experiment has afforded. It is perpetual-good for all years and with proper care will last a lifetime.

Egyptian Mystery Charms EGYPTIAN MYSTIC SCARF PINS AND RINGS The Originals of Which Were Brought Back from the Orient by

PROF. SEWARD IN HIS RECENT "WORLD TOUR" 

While in Cairo he visited the Bazaar District in the Native Quarters; here he came to the little Curio Shop of Mohamid 

Allah which, indeed, was a Mecca for the visitor who was in search for things antique; for here was a collection of rare jewels that would do honor to the dressing table of Cleopatra. 

A Picturesque old Arab in native costume made several gracious bows (salaamed) as he entered his shop-the Arab's manner at once assured him that he was a welcomed guest, after which his guide stated briefly in the native tongue just what Prof. Seward was most interested in, whereupon everything in the shop was pulled down for his inspection, among which was a Benaires brass tray filled with rare old Egyptian Coins, Charms, Bracelets, Scarabs and Rings of many designs. His eyes immediately fell upon. several old Egyptian Rings bearing the Zodiacal Symbols. No sooner had he began examining them than the old 

Arab assured him that they were "lucky rings" and would bring "glad tidings of joy and good fortune" to anyone who wore them and that the better things of life would be granted by Allah. 

Already knowing of the many apparently unexplainable wonders of the Orient he had no reason to dispute his belief. He also stated that the rings were found in excavating in an old temple near the Pyramids, and that they were most desirable specimens of antiquity and early Egyptian Art. 

Upon his return to America we engaged at considerable expense a large jawelry manufacturer who specializes in Masonic and Fraternity Emblems to make drawings and dies for the entire twelve Signs of the Zodiac in both rings and scarf pins. The workmanship is of a high quality, made of sterling silver and plated in green gold and should sell for at least $5.00. Being of special design it was necessary that we place an unusually large order for this Zodiacal Jewelry, but as we want each one of our patrons to own and wear one of these rings, we are making a special price of $2.50 for the rings and $1.50 for the scarf pins.

This jewelry being so strikingly odd it will be admired by everyone, and we have every reason to believe that you will be more than pleased with it.

In ordering rings, be sure to send a paper band indicating the size wanted, and also your date of birth.

These emblems are not to be had elsewhere, send your order direct-A. E. Seward & Co., 3620 Rokeby St., Chicago, Ill.
